# Support matrix

Want to check a particular analysis? Start with [supported analyses](supported-analyses.md).
Here, **licensed** means a supported execution path with recorded evidence and
limitations. It does not verify your causal assumptions or data support.

Generated by `scripts/generate_support_matrix_docs.py` from
`parity/support_axes.toml`, `parity/support_n_a.toml`,
`parity/support_closed.toml`, `parity/support_allowlist.toml`, and
`parity/support_licensed.toml`.
Do not edit this page by hand.

This page is the public **license**. `docs/capabilities.md` is an inventory
of what exists in the codebase; it does not license a cell.
See [ADR 0020](https://github.com/iridae-dev/antecedent/blob/main/adr/0020-support-matrix-and-prepared-workflow.md).

The Cartesian product (query × graph class × structure source × inference ×
validation) is **3402** cells. That denominator is not a feature count.
Of those cells, **1999** are typed impossibilities and
**1403** are meaningful combinations.

Every cell is in exactly **one of three runtime states**: **licensed** (a
result), **n/a** (the coordinate does not denote — a typed impossibility),
or **refused** (`SupportRefusal::Refused`). Refusal is the *default*: any
cell that is not licensed and not n/a is refused. `support_closed.toml`
does not close anything — it is the **reason table** for refused cells,
not a fourth state. 1.10 requires every refused cell to have a named
reason; a missing rule still refuses at runtime with the shared default
message. `allowed_unlicensed` is retained as a compatibility wire value,
but the current matrix has no active allowlist entries.

| Status | Count | How to read it |
|---|---|---|
| Cartesian product | 3402 | Axis product, not a coverage score |
| n/a | 1999 | Typed impossibilities (temporal query on a static graph, static query on a temporal graph, ATE-shaped cheap/full on a function-valued estimand, and similar). These are not holes. |
| Meaningful remainder | 1403 | Combinations that could in principle be a claim |
| Licensed | 463 | Staged path plus the row's recorded evidence contract and limitations |
| `allowed_unlicensed` compatibility entries | 0 | Retained wire value; the release gate requires this count to remain zero |
| Refused — reason on file | 940 | Same runtime outcome as any other refused cell; documented in legacy-named `support_closed.toml`, including mislabeled-inference laundering |
| Refused — no reason on file | 0 | Must stay 0; `gate_support_matrix.sh` fails if a refused cell has no `support_closed.toml` rule |

Do not read "463 / 3402" as coverage. Read: **463 cells
carry their recorded evidence contracts**; no cells run through the retained
`allowed_unlicensed` compatibility path; the rest are n/a or refused.

Static Frequentist `ResponseCurve` cells, and Frequentist `TemporalDag`
`ResponseCurve` / `InterventionResponse` at validation `none`, also require
the explicit [observation pair contract](observation-contract.md);
observation mechanisms are not a matrix axis. Unlicensed non-`Complete`
pairs refuse at compile.

A missing cell is refused, not unspecified. `analyze` is sugar over the
staged path; a combination that only works inside `analyze` cannot be
licensed. A cell is exactly one of licensed / n/a / refused.
`allowed_unlicensed` remains a readable wire value for compatibility with
older artifacts and clients, but no current matrix cell can produce it.

## Axes

**Queries** (root `__all__`):

- `AnomalyAttribution`
- `AverageDerivative`
- `AverageEffect`
- `ChangeAttribution`
- `ConditionalEffect`
- `Counterfactual`
- `DirectionalDerivative`
- `Elasticity`
- `InterventionalDistribution`
- `InterventionResponse`
- `MediationEffect`
- `PathSpecificEffect`
- `PointDerivative`
- `PulseEffect`
- `ResponseCurve`
- `ResponseJacobian`
- `SemiElasticity`
- `SustainedEffect`
- `TemporalMediationEffect`
- `TransportQuery`
- `InterferenceQuery`

**Graph classes** (`GraphClass`, plus classification-only `CoDetermined` / `Unknown`):

- `Dag`
- `Admg`
- `Cpdag`
- `Pag`
- `TemporalDag`
- `TemporalCpdag`
- `TemporalPag`
- `CoDetermined`
- `Unknown`

**Structure source:** `explicit`, `accepted`, `graph_posterior`.

**Inference:** `Frequentist`, `Bayesian`.

**Validation:** `none`, `cheap`, `full`.

## n/a

- queries ∈ {PulseEffect, SustainedEffect, TemporalMediationEffect} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag, Admg, Cpdag, Pag, CoDetermined, Unknown} — Temporal contrast queries require a temporal graph class.
- queries ∈ {AnomalyAttribution, AverageDerivative, AverageEffect, ChangeAttribution, ConditionalEffect, Counterfactual, DirectionalDerivative, Elasticity, InterventionalDistribution, MediationEffect, PathSpecificEffect, PointDerivative, ResponseJacobian, SemiElasticity} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {TemporalDag, TemporalCpdag, TemporalPag} — Static queries are not a temporal-graph cell; use PulseEffect, SustainedEffect, or a temporal ResponseCurve/InterventionResponse attachment.
- queries ∈ {TransportQuery, InterferenceQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {TemporalDag, TemporalCpdag, TemporalPag} — TransportQuery and InterferenceQuery are typed against static graphs; they do not denote on a temporal graph class.
- queries ∈ {ResponseCurve} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag, Admg, TemporalDag, Cpdag, Pag, TemporalCpdag, TemporalPag} ∧ structures ∈ {explicit, accepted} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — cheap and full denote the ATE-shaped scalar refuter suite; a function-valued curve has no such state.
- queries ∈ {ResponseCurve} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag, TemporalDag, TemporalCpdag, TemporalPag} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — cheap and full denote the ATE-shaped scalar refuter suite; a graph-posterior response remains function-valued or atom-mixed with no licensed scalar-refuter state.
- queries ∈ {InterventionResponse} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {TemporalDag, Cpdag, Pag, TemporalCpdag, TemporalPag} ∧ structures ∈ {explicit, accepted} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — cheap and full denote the ATE-shaped scalar refuter suite; temporal and class-aware InterventionResponse remain function-valued or envelope-mixed surfaces with no licensed scalar-refuter state.
- queries ∈ {InterventionResponse} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ structures ∈ {explicit, accepted, graph_posterior} ∧ inferences ∈ {Bayesian} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — Bayesian InterventionResponse has no licensed scalar-refuter state; cheap/full name the Frequentist ATE-shaped suite.
- queries ∈ {PointDerivative, Elasticity, SemiElasticity, AverageDerivative, DirectionalDerivative, ResponseJacobian} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ structures ∈ {explicit, accepted} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — Derivative-native validation is not implemented; ATE refuters do not apply.
- queries ∈ {AnomalyAttribution, AverageDerivative, ChangeAttribution, ConditionalEffect, Counterfactual, DirectionalDerivative, Elasticity, InterventionalDistribution, InterventionResponse, MediationEffect, PathSpecificEffect, PointDerivative, ResponseCurve, ResponseJacobian, SemiElasticity, TransportQuery, InterferenceQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Unknown} — Unknown-tier cells license the two-scenario AverageEffect envelope only.
- queries ∈ {AnomalyAttribution, AverageDerivative, ChangeAttribution, ConditionalEffect, Counterfactual, DirectionalDerivative, Elasticity, InterventionalDistribution, MediationEffect, PathSpecificEffect, PointDerivative, ResponseCurve, ResponseJacobian, SemiElasticity, TransportQuery, InterferenceQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {CoDetermined} — CoDetermined cells license AverageEffect and joint InterventionResponse only.
- graph_classes ∈ {CoDetermined, Unknown} ∧ structures ∈ {accepted, graph_posterior} — Tier-rule backgrounds are an explicit structure source.
- graph_classes ∈ {CoDetermined, Unknown} ∧ inferences ∈ {Bayesian} — Tier-rule cells are Frequentist (cell.aipw joint / two-scenario envelope).
- queries ∈ {AverageEffect, InterventionResponse} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {CoDetermined, Unknown} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — cheap and full name the ATE-shaped scalar refuter suite; CoDetermined joint IR and the Unknown envelope have no licensed scalar-refuter state.

## Refusal reasons

These cells are refused (wire id `refused`) — the default runtime state for
any cell that is not licensed and not n/a — and each row below is the
documented reason for that refusal. This is a reason table, not a fourth
state: an undocumented refused cell behaves identically, it just has no
row here yet.

- queries ∈ {Counterfactual} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg, Cpdag, Pag} — Counterfactual requires a supplied static Dag.
- queries ∈ {AverageDerivative, DirectionalDerivative, Elasticity, PointDerivative, ResponseJacobian, SemiElasticity} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg, Cpdag, Pag} — Derivatives require a supplied static Dag.
- queries ∈ {PathSpecificEffect, InterventionalDistribution} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag, Admg, Cpdag, Pag} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} — graph-posterior structures are refused: a path, distribution, or mediation mixture is not a single estimand across posterior atoms.
- queries ∈ {PathSpecificEffect} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg, Cpdag, Pag} ∧ structures ∈ {accepted} — Accepted path queries require a Dag.
- queries ∈ {InterventionalDistribution} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Cpdag, Pag} ∧ structures ∈ {accepted} — Accepted InterventionalDistribution is licensed on Dag and Admg; Cpdag/Pag remain refused.
- queries ∈ {MediationEffect} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg, Cpdag, Pag} — MediationEffect requires a supplied static Dag.
- queries ∈ {AnomalyAttribution, ChangeAttribution} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg, Cpdag, Pag} — AnomalyAttribution and ChangeAttribution require a supplied static Dag.
- queries ∈ {AnomalyAttribution, ChangeAttribution} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ structures ∈ {accepted, graph_posterior} — Attribution is licensed only for an explicit Dag at validation none.
- queries ∈ {AnomalyAttribution, ChangeAttribution} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ inferences ∈ {Bayesian} — Attribution has no Bayesian draw path; the licensed cells are Frequentist.
- queries ∈ {AnomalyAttribution, ChangeAttribution} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — Attribution has no native refuter suite; ATE refuters do not apply.
- queries ∈ {TransportQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag, Cpdag, Pag} — Licensed TransportQuery is Admg-only; selection diagrams are ADMGs. Recursive sID outside the implemented subset is NotCertified.
- queries ∈ {TransportQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg} ∧ structures ∈ {accepted} — TransportQuery is licensed only for an explicit Admg at validation none.
- queries ∈ {TransportQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} ∧ inferences ∈ {Bayesian} — TransportQuery graph-posterior mixtures are not staged.
- queries ∈ {TransportQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg} ∧ inferences ∈ {Bayesian} — TransportQuery has no Bayesian trial-to-target estimator.
- queries ∈ {TransportQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — TransportQuery has no native refuter suite; ATE refuters do not apply.
- queries ∈ {InterferenceQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg, Cpdag, Pag} — Licensed InterferenceQuery is Dag-only; the graph binds schema/outcome, it does not identify the contrast.
- queries ∈ {InterferenceQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ structures ∈ {accepted} — InterferenceQuery is licensed only for an explicit Dag at validation none.
- queries ∈ {InterferenceQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} ∧ inferences ∈ {Bayesian} — InterferenceQuery graph-posterior mixtures are not staged.
- queries ∈ {InterferenceQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ inferences ∈ {Bayesian} — InterferenceQuery is design-based Frequentist; there is no Bayesian randomization estimator.
- queries ∈ {InterferenceQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — InterferenceQuery has no native refuter suite; ATE refuters do not apply.
- queries ∈ {ConditionalEffect} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg} — ConditionalEffect on Admg has no compile arm; Dag, Cpdag, and Pag are licensed.
- queries ∈ {PathSpecificEffect} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg, Pag} ∧ structures ∈ {explicit} — Path-specific queries execute only on a supplied static Dag; a directly supplied Admg/Pag hits the same static-Dag requirement (accepted/graph-posterior Admg and Pag are already refused above).
- queries ∈ {InterventionalDistribution} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Pag} ∧ structures ∈ {explicit} — Path and distribution queries execute only on a supplied static Dag or Admg; a directly supplied Pag hits the static-Dag/Admg requirement.
- queries ∈ {InterventionalDistribution} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg} ∧ structures ∈ {explicit} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — ADMG InterventionalDistribution is licensed at validation none; cheap/full remain Dag-only.
- queries ∈ {TemporalMediationEffect} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {TemporalPag} — Latent-confounded TemporalPag natural mediation is outside the existing cross-world identification theory; DAG-completion engineering cannot license it.
- queries ∈ {AverageDerivative, Counterfactual, DirectionalDerivative, Elasticity, InterventionalDistribution, MediationEffect, PathSpecificEffect, PointDerivative, ResponseJacobian, SemiElasticity, TransportQuery, InterferenceQuery} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag, Admg, Cpdag, Pag} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} ∧ inferences ∈ {Frequentist} — Frequentist graph-posterior mixing on DAG atoms is licensed for AverageEffect, ConditionalEffect, and static ResponseCurve/one-coordinate InterventionResponse. The listed static families remain unsupported.
- queries ∈ {TemporalMediationEffect} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {TemporalPag} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} ∧ inferences ∈ {Frequentist, Bayesian} — Latent-confounded TemporalPag natural mediation is outside the existing cross-world identification theory; TemporalPag graph-posterior atoms are not mixed into a mediation envelope.
- queries ∈ {PathSpecificEffect, InterventionalDistribution} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Cpdag} ∧ structures ∈ {explicit} — Path-specific queries execute only on a supplied static Dag and distribution queries only on a supplied static Dag or Admg; a directly supplied Cpdag hits that requirement (Admg/Pag explicit already named above).
- queries ∈ {PathSpecificEffect, InterventionalDistribution} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Cpdag, Admg, Pag} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — Query-native functional validation requires a Dag.
- queries ∈ {AverageDerivative, DirectionalDerivative, Elasticity, PointDerivative, ResponseJacobian, SemiElasticity} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} ∧ inferences ∈ {Bayesian} — Graph-posterior derivative mixtures are not staged; the licensed derivative cells are Frequentist or Bayesian explicit or accepted Dag at validation none.
- queries ∈ {MediationEffect} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} ∧ inferences ∈ {Bayesian} — Graph-posterior mediation mixtures are not staged; the licensed MediationEffect cells are Frequentist or Bayesian explicit or accepted Dag.
- queries ∈ {Counterfactual} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} — Staged counterfactuals require a supplied Dag; graph-posterior structures are refused.
- queries ∈ {Counterfactual} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Dag} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — Counterfactual cheap/full are not licensed; there is no native ITE refuter suite and ATE refuters do not apply.
- queries ∈ {ResponseCurve} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Admg, Cpdag, Pag} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — Graph-posterior ResponseCurve on Admg/Cpdag/Pag is licensed at validation none. cheap/full name the ATE-shaped scalar refuter suite; the curve remains function-valued or atom-mixed with no licensed scalar-refuter state.
- queries ∈ {InterventionResponse} ∧ graph_classes ∈ {Cpdag, Pag} ∧ structures ∈ {graph_posterior} ∧ inferences ∈ {Bayesian} ∧ validations ∈ {cheap, full} — Bayesian graph-posterior InterventionResponse on Cpdag/Pag is licensed at validation none. cheap/full attach the Frequentist plugin-level suite; there is no Bayesian class-IR refuter path on a static Cpdag/Pag posterior.

## `allowed_unlicensed` compatibility entries

The wire value is retained for compatibility with older artifacts and clients.
The release gate requires this list to be empty: every active cell is licensed,
n/a, or refused.

_None._

## Licensed cells

The **evidence** column is the row's own evidence: the test that runs the whole
route. The **external oracles** column keeps two kinds apart. *Cell output
compared with* means the cell's own result is checked against a pinned upstream
run. *Component oracles* means a component the cell's execution ran (its
identifier or estimator, from `parity/licensed_routes.toml`, generated from the
executed plans) is checked against an external oracle by that parity row; the
cell's own output is not. *Discovery oracles* apply when an accepted graph of
that class came from that discovery strategy.

Of 463 licensed cells, 6 compare their own output with an external oracle; 144 more run an identifier or estimator that an external oracle checks; 106 more are accepted-graph cells whose class a discovery oracle backs; 207 rest on internal evidence only.

| query | graph | structure | inference | validation | evidence | external oracles | limitations |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ResponseCurve` | `Dag` | `graph_posterior` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Each DAG atom is identified and estimated independently. Posterior graph weights, atom values, identified/unidentified/unevaluable mass, the pointwise identified set, and the conditional-on-identified mean are retained. Failed estimation is unevaluable, not unidentified: the atom keeps its identification status with no value, its mass is reported as unevaluable_mass (also in Python), and any unevaluable mass makes the result GraphDependent. Prepared clicks reuse the graph-posterior identification cache. The joint-IF SE is published only for a scalar aggregate; a multi-atom curve withholds its band (`estimate.response.graph_posterior.uncertainty_withheld`). Prepared handles refuse non-complete-observation queries before identification. Simultaneous bands are not claimed. Known-truth pin (conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures static_response): atom curves 1 + 2.75t and 1 + 2t at weights 0.5 / 0.3 with 0.2 unidentified give conditional_on_identified = 1 + 2.46875t; this is a numeric pin, not a coverage claim. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |
| `PulseEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Each identified DBN atom uses its own I(h), with fixed posterior graph weights and unidentified mass retained. When requested, uncertainty refits every contributing atom on the same circular-block replicate before mixing; it is not a sum of atom variances. Frequentist uncertainty (when replicates are requested) is a shared circular-block bootstrap over lag-aligned series times: blocks of consecutive times are resampled over the window where every atom's maximal lag window is available, each atom's lag-aligned design keeps its rows' original lag windows (no row pairs an outcome with lags from an unrelated block), every contributing atom is refit on the same resampled times, and the replicate SD is scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor. Block length is max(span, ⌈m^{1/3}⌉) over the m shared times (span = max lag + 1), lengthened to ⌈b_PW·m^{1/6}⌉ (the Politis-White length at the fixed-b testing rate) when any atom's influence, the mixture score, or any fitted regression's normal-equation score (residuals included) is persistently dependent; estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series warns when the score effective rows (the smallest over every atom's and the mixture's score of the lag-1 and block-length readings) fall below 155 (docs/short-series-thresholds.md). That threshold covers a bias the block bootstrap cannot remove: at ρ ≥ 0.9 an atom that omits a persistent confounder is biased in finite samples (in the docs/short-series-thresholds.md sweep, v19_short_series_measurement, TemporalCpdag Pulse with two completions measured 0.685–0.905 (not a registry value) at ρ ≥ 0.9, n ≤ 400 and DBN multi-step Sustained 0.859–0.862 (not a registry value) at ρ = 0.95, n ≤ 60: a warned boundary). Coverage (heterogeneous atoms, iid and AR(1)): crates/antecedent/tests/v19_calibration.rs. Python reaches this cell through analyze(discovery=DbnPosterior(...) or GraphPosterior(...), inference=Frequentist()) and PreparedAnalysis.prepare(discovery=...); the shared-block replicate count follows an explicit bootstrap or the latency tier (interactive: 0, SE withheld). The result carries structural_response (posterior-probability atoms, identified and unidentified mass, identified set). |
| `PulseEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Frequentist` | `cheap` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Applicable temporal refuters run on every contributing atom against that atom's own estimate (not the pooled mean) and mix by fixed graph weight; the mixed check passes only if every atom passes. They do not rediscover graphs. Pulse is a single intervention time; multi-step Sustained validation is documented in its own cells. |
| `PulseEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Frequentist` | `full` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Applicable temporal refuters run on every contributing atom against that atom's own estimate (not the pooled mean) and mix by fixed graph weight; the mixed check passes only if every atom passes. They do not rediscover graphs. Pulse is a single intervention time; multi-step Sustained validation is documented in its own cells. |
| `SustainedEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Frequentist` | `cheap` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Applicable temporal refuters run on every contributing atom against that atom's own estimate (not the pooled mean) and mix by fixed graph weight; the mixed check passes only if every atom passes. | `PulseEffect` | `TemporalCpdag` | `graph_posterior` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Each TemporalCpdag posterior atom is reconstructed from adjacency + lag_masks and evaluated with the existing temporal class envelope (completion enumeration is not posterior probability). | `TemporalMediationEffect` | `TemporalCpdag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_cross_check (`crates/antecedent/tests/temporal_mediation_horizons.rs::temporal_cpdag_mediation_returns_per_horizon_identified_sets`) | component oracles: `estimate.temporal_mediation.external_parity` (estimator:temporal.mediation) | The existing linear temporal mediation identifier runs independently on every valid TemporalDag completion and horizon, each completion adjusting its own S(h) = I(h) plus its mediator-outcome confounders. Completion-specific effects are returned as identified sets, never an enumeration-weighted mean. TemporalPag latent-confounded natural mediation is not claimed. When every completion is identified and fits the same mediation design (same estimand and same S(h)), the identified set is one contrast and the horizon slice publishes that design's shared circular-block bootstrap SE, the TemporalDag route's construction on the same stream (crates/antecedent/tests/v110_calibration_temporal.rs::temporal_cpdag_mediation_slice_interval_is_the_shared_design_interval). At a single requested horizon that contrast is the result's own estimate and mediation record too, so the reported interval is that circular-block SE beside the identified set (crates/antecedent/tests/v110_calibration_temporal.rs::temporal_cpdag_mediation_class_reports_the_circular_block_interval). Completions that fit different designs, an unidentified or capped completion, and several requested horizons keep an unbanded identified set: per-completion iid analytic SEs are NaN. |

| `ResponseCurve` | `TemporalCpdag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_cross_check (`crates/antecedent/tests/temporal_class_envelope_numeric_pins.rs::temporal_class_response_returns_completion_identified_set`) | none | Per horizon x completion: TemporalResponseEstimator (curves, Set/Shift/Soft) or unfolded sequential g-comp (multi-step / joint Sequence, Soft multiplicative and truncated_shift) on each identified completion. The published object is a completion-enumeration identified set: pointwise min/max over completion point surfaces, with no weighted mean and no class-level sampling band. Requested replicates ride each completion atom as that atom's own joint circular-block pointwise band plus simultaneous band (response.simultaneous_band.*); their coverage is calibrated against each atom's own probability limit, not a class truth (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_response_calibration.rs). Numeric pins: identified-set lower/upper and every atom equal an independent lag-aligned OLS g-computation under that atom's adjustment set (temporal_class_response_returns_completion_identified_set, ResponseCurve and Set/Shift InterventionResponse). Licensed 1.3/1.6 observation pairs ride per completion: curve atoms use the tuple-level outer circular-block bootstrap; a multi-completion identified set withholds the class band; observation-adjusted Sequence atoms refit the nuisance and every unfolded sequential mechanism on the same resampled tuples. Nested Sequence stays refused; bidirected MAG completions stay unevaluable for sequential g-comp (mass retained). Delayed entry, interval, and truncation stay refused. Evidence also: temporal_class_observation.rs, temporal_class_sequence_policies.rs. |

Nested Sequence stays refused; bidirected MAG completions stay unevaluable for sequential g-comp (mass retained). Delayed entry, interval, and truncation stay refused. Evidence also: temporal_class_observation.rs, temporal_class_sequence_policies.rs. |
| `SustainedEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Single- and multi-step sustained policies use per-atom temporal adjustment/sequential g-computation. Fixed graph weights are mixed inside each shared circular-block replicate. Multi-step cheap/full are exercised in crates/antecedent/tests/dbn_sustained_validation.rs, including actual sequential perturbations and circular-block bootstrap checks. Frequentist uncertainty (when replicates are requested) is a shared circular-block bootstrap over lag-aligned series times: blocks of consecutive times are resampled over the window where every atom's maximal lag window is available, each atom's lag-aligned design keeps its rows' original lag windows (no row pairs an outcome with lags from an unrelated block), every contributing atom is refit on the same resampled times, and the replicate SD is scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor. Block length is max(span, ⌈m^{1/3}⌉) over the m shared times (span = max lag + 1), lengthened to ⌈b_PW·m^{1/6}⌉ (the Politis-White length at the fixed-b testing rate) when any atom's influence, the mixture score, or any fitted regression's normal-equation score (residuals included) is persistently dependent; estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series warns when the score effective rows (the smallest over every atom's and the mixture's score of the lag-1 and block-length readings) fall below 155 (docs/short-series-thresholds.md). That threshold covers a bias the block bootstrap cannot remove: at ρ ≥ 0.9 an atom that omits a persistent confounder is biased in finite samples (in the docs/short-series-thresholds.md sweep, v19_short_series_measurement, TemporalCpdag Pulse with two completions measured 0.685–0.905 (not a registry value) at ρ ≥ 0.9, n ≤ 400 and DBN multi-step Sustained 0.859–0.862 (not a registry value) at ρ = 0.95, n ≤ 60: a warned boundary). Coverage (heterogeneous atoms, iid and AR(1)): crates/antecedent/tests/v19_calibration.rs. Python reaches this cell through analyze(discovery=DbnPosterior(...) or GraphPosterior(...), inference=Frequentist()) and PreparedAnalysis.prepare(discovery=...); the shared-block replicate count follows an explicit bootstrap or the latency tier (interactive: 0, SE withheld). The result carries structural_response (posterior-probability atoms, identified and unidentified mass, identified set). |

Frozen posterior weights mix the atom surfaces (`StructuralAggregationPolicy`); unidentified mass is retained and makes the result GraphDependent. Failed estimation is unevaluable, not unidentified. Completion enumeration is not used (atoms are DAGs). Multi-atom aggregate bands are withheld (`estimate.response.dbn_posterior.uncertainty_withheld`). | `ResponseCurve` | `Dag` | `graph_posterior` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Per-atom Bayesian g-computation supplies atom point responses. Posterior graph weights and all mass categories are retained; the conditional-on-identified point mean is probability-weighted. Failed estimation is unevaluable, not unidentified: the atom keeps its identification status with no value, its mass is reported as unevaluable_mass (also in Python), and any unevaluable mass makes the result GraphDependent. Prepared clicks reuse the identification cache. Multi-atom Bayesian aggregate intervals are withheld (`estimate.response.graph_posterior.uncertainty_withheld`): graph-specific dispersion and a frozen-weight aggregate are different objects. Known-truth pin (conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures static_response): atom curves 1 + 2.75t and 1 + 2t at weights 0.5 / 0.3 with 0.2 unidentified give conditional_on_identified = 1 + 2.46875t; this is a numeric pin, not a coverage claim. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |
| `InterventionResponse` | `Dag` | `graph_posterior` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | One intervention coordinate only. Atom values, posterior graph weights, mass categories, pointwise identified set, and conditional-on-identified mean are retained. Failed estimation is unevaluable, not unidentified: the atom keeps its identification status with no value, its mass is reported as unevaluable_mass (also in Python), and any unevaluable mass makes the result GraphDependent. Prepared clicks reuse the identification cache. A Frequentist multi-atom frozen-weight aggregate publishes a joint-IF SE from atom influences aligned on the shared data rows by complete-case row index (`estimate.response.graph_posterior.joint_if_se`), at response_options.confidence_level; response options apply to every atom; without aligned influences the interval is withheld (`estimate.response.graph_posterior.uncertainty_withheld`). The interval is not coverage-calibrated. Prepared handles refuse multi-coordinate or non-complete-observation queries before identification. Simultaneous bands are not claimed. Known-truth pin (conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures static_response): atom curves 1 + 2.75t and 1 + 2t at weights 0.5 / 0.3 with 0.2 unidentified give conditional_on_identified = 1 + 2.46875t; this is a numeric pin, not a coverage claim. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |

| `AverageEffect` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/linear_gaussian_ate`) | component oracles: `estimate.identify.backdoor` (identifier:backdoor.adjustment), `estimate.linear_regression` (estimator:linear.adjustment.ate) | Propensity-score estimators (weighting, matching, stratification, distance matching, AIPW) clip propensities into [0.01, 0.99] and trim nothing by default; a configured `overlap` clip/trim changes the inference binding and the calibration key, so it reports `scope_not_assessed` unless a record measured that policy. The estimator is not a matrix axis; IPW / IV / front-door / rd.sharp ride this cell with their own parity fixtures in parity/estimate.toml (rd.sharp requires rd_config; fixture conformance/estimate/rd_sharp). Estimator SEs riding this cell are gated at 400 replicates, nominal 95%, two-sided 0.95 ± 3 MCSE (crates/antecedent-estimate/src/calibration_coverage.rs): linear adjustment 0.943 / HC1 0.945, Hajek IPW analytic 0.935 and 0.953 on the propensity_ipw conformance SCM at n = 1200 (mean SE 0.0416 vs Monte Carlo SD 0.0410; the fixture draw's 0.074 is a high-variance draw whose bootstrap SE is 0.080), cross-fitted AIPW 0.973, residualized AIPW ATE/ATT/ATC HC1 0.948/0.958/0.925 and ATT cluster 0.943, Wald IV 0.973 / HC1 0.945, 2SLS 0.960 / HC1 under heteroskedasticity 0.945, stacked front-door HC0/HC1 0.930/0.938, matching 0.953. Since 1.9 residualized AIPW ATT/ATC use the centered efficient influence function (the plug-in IF measured 0.980 ATT / 0.863 ATC). rd.sharp's default analytic SE is the HC1 residual sandwich (assumption rd.sharp.conventional_robust_se; 0.965 when the variance grows along the running variable; numpy reference pin conformance/estimate/rd_sharp se_reference); the classical homoskedastic SE is an explicit opt-in via RdConfig::with_se_kind(Homoskedastic) through StudyBuilder::rd_design (assumption rd.sharp.homoskedastic_se; 0.968 on a homoskedastic law, 0.993 under the heteroskedastic law). Python selects the same kinds through estimator_config={'se_kind': ...} or SharpRd(se=...). |

For the non-linear estimators riding this cell (AIPW, IPW, matching, stratification, front-door, IV, rd.sharp) full reduces to overlap and the overlap rule, E-value, nonparametric sensitivity, and Riesz sensitivity (binary treatment only): placebo, dummy outcome, random and unobserved common cause, bootstrap CI coverage, data subset, drop-covariate graph, and linear/partial-linear sensitivity are NotApplicable because they refit the linear back-door path (antecedent-validate suite.rs). |

The estimator is not a matrix axis; IPW / IV / front-door / rd.sharp ride this cell with their own parity fixtures in parity/estimate.toml (rd.sharp requires rd_config; fixture conformance/estimate/rd_sharp). Estimator SEs riding this cell are gated at 400 replicates, nominal 95%, two-sided 0.95 ± 3 MCSE (crates/antecedent-estimate/src/calibration_coverage.rs): linear adjustment 0.943 / HC1 0.945, Hajek IPW analytic 0.935 and 0.953 on the propensity_ipw conformance SCM at n = 1200 (mean SE 0.0416 vs Monte Carlo SD 0.0410; the fixture draw's 0.074 is a high-variance draw whose bootstrap SE is 0.080), cross-fitted AIPW 0.973, residualized AIPW ATE/ATT/ATC HC1 0.948/0.958/0.925 and ATT cluster 0.943, Wald IV 0.973 / HC1 0.945, 2SLS 0.960 / HC1 under heteroskedasticity 0.945, stacked front-door HC0/HC1 0.930/0.938, matching 0.953. Python selects the same kinds through estimator_config={'se_kind': ...} or SharpRd(se=...). |

| `ResponseCurve` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/two_point_curve_average_effect`) | none | The two-point curve contrast and AverageEffect are each compared to the fixture's analytic linear-SCM truth, and are cross-checked against each other. This is a shared correctly specified linear contract, not nonlinear response-curve validation. Observation pairs are explicitly constrained by docs/observation-contract.md: selected AIPW with OutcomeIndependentGiven(Z), marginal right/left KM with IndependentGiven([]), conditional right/left Cox with IndependentGiven(Z). Fixtures: conformance/response/observation_pairs and conformance/response/conditional_ipcw. Conditional delayed entry, interval/truncation MLE and joint observation/curve bands are unavailable. Since 1.9 the pointwise Kennedy-DR SE includes the covariate-marginalization influence term (Kennedy et al. 2017, Thm. 3); without it the pointwise and simultaneous bands under-covered (that pre-fix run was not retained). 400-replicate 90% coverage on a confounded linear law: pointwise 0.882–0.890 (boundary records `cov.response_curve.dag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.response_curve_dag_frequentist_pointwise_nominal_90_coverage.a_1`, `cov.response_curve.dag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.response_curve_dag_frequentist_pointwise_nominal_90_coverage.a__0_5`, `cov.response_curve.dag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.response_curve_dag_frequentist_pointwise_nominal_90_coverage.a__1`) over a five-point grid, sup-t multiplier simultaneous band 0.855 (record `cov.response_curve.dag.frequentist.simultaneous_band.l90.response_curve_dag_frequentist_simultaneous_nominal_90_coverage`) (coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_calibration.rs::response_curve_dag_frequentist_pointwise_nominal_90_coverage, ::response_curve_dag_frequentist_simultaneous_nominal_90_coverage, calibration gate); a heavy-tailed density-ratio probe measured 0.877–0.897 (not a registry value). The simultaneous critical value is floored at the pointwise z. Bands condition on the fitted nuisances and bandwidth. |

| `ResponseCurve` | `TemporalDag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/temporal_dose_horizon`) | none | TemporalResponseSpec is required. Primary pin is temporal_dose_horizon. Identification (Z@-1, temporal.backdoor.unfolded) and the structural estimate are additionally pinned on temporal_confounded_pulse; per-horizon empirical support on temporal_horizon_support. Licensed 1.3 observation pairs (Selected × OutcomeIndependentGiven, left/right KM and Cox IPCW) ride this cell and consume conformance/response/temporal_observation, not observation_primitives. IndependentGiven(Z) lag-aligns Z at the policy treatment offset. Unlicensed non-Complete pairs refuse at compile. Delayed entry, interval/truncation, and cheap/full stay refused. TemporalCpdag/Pag observation rides the same licensed pairs per completion and consumes conformance/response/temporal_class_observation; complete-data bands are not reused. Bayesian observation uses its own Gaussian observed-data likelihood route, not IPCW. Uncertainty: with bootstrap replicates (Study default 199) pointwise SEs come from a joint circular-block bootstrap of lag-aligned rows (block max(max(unfolded span, ceil(sqrt(n))), min(ceil(b_PW·n^(1/6)), n/3)), b_PW the largest Politis-White length over the level's estimating scores (normal-equation scores and centered covariate columns), recorded as response.temporal.block_length; every horizon refit on the same resampled calendar times over their common window, covariate averages recomputed per replicate, deviations scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b critical-value ratio for b = block/rows and the HC1 factor, and by each cell's kernel-bias factor 1/sqrt(f) (response.temporal.kernel_bias_factor), f the share of the long-run variance of the autoregression fitted to that cell's influence (Kendall AR(1), or the BIC-selected AR(q<=4) when larger) that the block's Bartlett kernel keeps; response.temporal.effective_rows carries each cell's effective rows and response.temporal.block.short_series warns below 15); the pointwise band is mean +/- 1.96 SE for the population level E[Y_h \| do(A)], and a simultaneous band over the whole dose x horizon grid (max-studentized deviation, at least 40 replicates) is published next to it as support diagnostics response.simultaneous_band.{lower,upper,critical}. With zero replicates no band is published (estimate.temporal_response.band_withheld): the analytic homoskedastic band would treat lag-aligned rows as independent. Observation-adjusted curves and single Set/Shift responses: the outer circular-block bootstrap resamples lag-aligned outcome-time tuples, refits the observation nuisance and every horizon, and publishes pointwise (mean +/- 1.96 SE of the same fixed-b-scaled replicates) and simultaneous bands. Repeated-sampling coverage (400 replicates, n=160, pointwise per cell and simultaneous per surface) is gated on iid and AR(1) rho=0.5 residuals and on both the dose curve (0.945-0.948 / 0.948 (not a registry value)) and the shift response (0.943 / 0.948 (not a registry value); 0.910 / 0.912 (not a registry value) before the kernel-bias factor; about ten effective rows, short-series-warned) under an AR(1) phi=0.9 treatment; AR(1) rho=0.9 residuals (0.887-0.943 pointwise, 0.877 (not a registry value) simultaneous at n=160; 0.900-0.922 / 0.895 (not a registry value) at n=400; 0.902-0.943 / 0.905 (not a registry value) at n=1000) remain an unasserted boundary record, disclosed on every band as response.temporal.block.persistence_boundary: the persistent part is 15% of the residual and its long-run ratio is not estimable at these n: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_response_calibration.rs. |

| `AverageEffect` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/shared_functional_ate`) | component oracles: `estimate.identify.backdoor` (identifier:backdoor.adjustment) | The Bayesian posterior mean is compared directly to the fixture's structural ATE and additionally cross-checked against frequentist linear adjustment. Backend posteriors are pinned separately (conformance/bayesian/conjugate_gaussian, laplace_glm). The hmc backend floors under-specified draw counts to clear the MCMC publication gate. bayesian.gcomp declares a Gaussian identity-link outcome regression linear in the treatment and each adjustment column with no treatment × covariate interaction (assumption bayesian.gcomp.linear_gaussian_outcome); its credible interval is for that model's treatment contrast. `Bayesian(likelihood=logit\|probit\|poisson)` fits a Bernoulli or Poisson GLM on this cell instead (the assumption names that family) and averages the inverse-link contrast over the rows; every other Bayesian cell, the conjugate backend and a transferred prior refuse a non-Gaussian likelihood with `likelihood_not_supported`, and a Gaussian fit to a 0/1 or nonnegative-integer outcome reports `estimate.bayesian.gaussian_likelihood_discrete_outcome`. 400-replicate 90% coverage 0.890 (record `cov.average_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_gcomp_misspecification_probe.linear_outcome`) on a linear law; R-17 misspecification probes measured 0.705 (not a registry value) with an omitted z² term (bias 0.154) and 0.215 (not a registry value) with an omitted t·z interaction (bias 0.282) (coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_calibration.rs::bayesian_gcomp_misspecification_probe, calibration gate). |

Prior sensitivity perturbs the prior in force (variance-multiplier grid around a staged or transferred prior, alpha grid for a prior-bank compose, isotropic scale grid only when no prior was supplied; the summary records the family) and the prior PPC simulates from that prior. bayesian.gcomp declares a Gaussian identity-link outcome regression linear in the treatment and each adjustment column with no treatment × covariate interaction (assumption bayesian.gcomp.linear_gaussian_outcome); its credible interval is for that model's treatment contrast. `Bayesian(likelihood=logit\|probit\|poisson)` fits a Bernoulli or Poisson GLM on this cell instead (the assumption names that family) and averages the inverse-link contrast over the rows; every other Bayesian cell, the conjugate backend and a transferred prior refuse a non-Gaussian likelihood with `likelihood_not_supported`, and a Gaussian fit to a 0/1 or nonnegative-integer outcome reports `estimate.bayesian.gaussian_likelihood_discrete_outcome`. 400-replicate 90% coverage 0.890 (record `cov.average_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_gcomp_misspecification_probe.linear_outcome`) on a linear law; R-17 misspecification probes measured 0.705 (not a registry value) with an omitted z² term (bias 0.154) and 0.215 (not a registry value) with an omitted t·z interaction (bias 0.282) (v19_static_calibration::bayesian_gcomp_misspecification_probe). |
| `ConditionalEffect` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/context/conditional_effect`) | component oracles: `estimate.identify.backdoor` (identifier:backdoor.adjustment), `estimate.conditional` (estimator:conditional.linear.adjustment) | Frequentist linear interaction regression reporting b_t + b_tx * mean(modifier), the effect averaged over one modifier's distribution; since 1.9 its SE includes the sampling variance of the modifier mean. AllObserved. 400-replicate 90% coverage 0.882 (record `cov.conditional_effect.dag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.conditional_effect_dag_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) (v19_static_envelope_calibration::conditional_effect_dag_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage), and 0.885 (record `cov.conditional_effect.dag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.conditional_effect_dag_frequentist_small_subgroup_nominal_90_coverage`) with a rare modifier level (8% of n=300) (v19_static_envelope_calibration::conditional_effect_dag_frequentist_small_subgroup_nominal_90_coverage). cheap/full are licensed separately and run the effect-refuter suite on this scalar, refitting the interaction model. Bayesian inference uses a separate conditional.bayesian estimator. Exceedance and quantile outcome functionals on binary 0/1 arms are computed from cross-fitted AIPW scores (modifier added to the adjustment set), not the interaction model; the result says so (estimate.conditional.crossfit_aipw_scores). |

| `PathSpecificEffect` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/functional_validation`) | none | Discrete natural path functional on a supplied or accepted Dag; selected path and cross-world identification assumptions remain frozen. Evidence: conformance/estimate/functional_validation through the staged handle (functional_validation_staged_known_truth); conformance/context/path_specific_natural is pinned by the crate's end_to_end_path_specific_natural_effect test. cheap/full are licensed separately with path-contrast subset stability diagnostics, not ATE refuters or calibrated causal-validity tests. Since 1.9 a directed treatment–outcome path outside the selected set is handled by the edge g-formula (Avin, Shpitser & Pearl 2005; Shpitser 2013): each P(v \| pa(v)) over the outcome's ancestors takes the treatment at the active level on edges that start a selected path and at the control level elsewhere, minus the all-control g-formula; when every path is selected the effect is the total effect and general ID runs on the surgical graph. A recanting witness (a treatment child that starts both a selected and an unselected directed path, which is exactly the Avin–Shpitser–Pearl criterion; a node shared by paths that leave the treatment through different children is not one) and a truncated path enumeration are refused as not identified. Known truth: conformance/estimate/path_specific_edge_gformula (path effect 0.12, total 0.356; shared-descendant case path effect 0.125, total 0.0625; path_specific_edge_gformula_pins). 400-replicate 90% coverage: chain 0.887 (record `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.frequentist.bootstrap_se.l90.path_specific_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) (Frequentist bootstrap) and 0.895 (record `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.path_specific_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`) (Bayesian); two-path confounded law 0.882 and 0.882 (records `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.path_specific_two_path_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`, `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.frequentist.bootstrap_se.l90.path_specific_two_path_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) (coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_calibration.rs::path_specific_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage, ::path_specific_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage, ::path_specific_two_path_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage, ::path_specific_two_path_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage, calibration gate). |
| `InterventionResponse` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/intervention_response`) | none | response.intervention_gcomp only; Set/Shift/Bernoulli/Gaussian/Categorical interventions are supported, Soft and Sequence interventions require a structural/temporal model and are refused. The fixture pins a hard Set intervention; the g-computation strategy is shared across all supported intervention kinds (see response.rs::intervention_response), so this is the representative cell. Since 1.9 the treatment splines of the g-computation target are unpenalized regression splines (a quantile-knot roughness penalty biased a linear dose effect by about half an SE and under-covered; that pre-fix run was not retained); binary or low-cardinality treatments keep the penalized fit. 400-replicate 90% coverage 0.885 (record `cov.intervention_response.dag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.intervention_response_dag_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) (coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_calibration.rs::intervention_response_dag_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage, calibration gate). cell.aipw joint cell mean 95% interval with a multinomial-logit assignment law: 0.935 (record `cov.intervention_response.dag.frequentist.analytic_se.l95.intervention_response_cell_aipw_nominal_95_coverage`) (intervention_response_cell_aipw_nominal_95_coverage). |

| `InterventionResponse` | `TemporalDag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/temporal_dose_horizon`) | none | TemporalResponseSpec is required. Known-truth execution covers Set(1), Soft(constant=1), Soft(additive_shift=1), single-step Sequence, and multi-step Sequence([Set(1), Set(1)]) → [6, 4] on temporal_dose_horizon (same unfolded sequential g-comp as multi-step Sustained; identifier remains temporal.backdoor.unfolded; not a last-step collapse). Joint single-time Sequence is licensed when every coordinate is identified. Soft multiplicative scales the structural assignment. Soft truncated_shift defines the population-mean-targeting shift f_policy=f+clip(mu+delta,l,u)-mu, mu=E[f] under preceding interventions, preserving parent effects and innovations; outcomes are not clipped. Extra-family evidence: conformance/response/temporal_soft_mean_mechanisms. Nested Sequence remains refused. Identification (Z@-1, temporal.backdoor.unfolded) and the structural estimate are additionally pinned on temporal_confounded_pulse; per-horizon support on temporal_horizon_support. Licensed 1.3 observation pairs ride Set/Soft/Sequence on this cell and consume conformance/response/temporal_observation (right-censor Set(0.5) pin). Unlicensed non-Complete pairs refuse at compile. Uncertainty: with bootstrap replicates (Study default 199) pointwise SEs come from a joint circular-block bootstrap of lag-aligned rows (block max(max(unfolded span, ceil(sqrt(n))), min(ceil(b_PW·n^(1/6)), n/3)), b_PW the largest Politis-White length over the level's estimating scores (normal-equation scores and centered covariate columns), recorded as response.temporal.block_length; every horizon refit on the same resampled calendar times over their common window, covariate averages recomputed per replicate, deviations scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b critical-value ratio for b = block/rows and the HC1 factor, and by each cell's kernel-bias factor 1/sqrt(f) (response.temporal.kernel_bias_factor), f the share of the long-run variance of the autoregression fitted to that cell's influence (Kendall AR(1), or the BIC-selected AR(q<=4) when larger) that the block's Bartlett kernel keeps; response.temporal.effective_rows carries each cell's effective rows and response.temporal.block.short_series warns below 15); the pointwise band is mean +/- 1.96 SE for the population level E[Y_h \| do(A)], and a simultaneous band over the whole dose x horizon grid (max-studentized deviation, at least 40 replicates) is published next to it as support diagnostics response.simultaneous_band.{lower,upper,critical}. With zero replicates no band is published (estimate.temporal_response.band_withheld): the analytic homoskedastic band would treat lag-aligned rows as independent. Observation-adjusted curves and single Set/Shift responses: the outer circular-block bootstrap resamples lag-aligned outcome-time tuples, refits the observation nuisance and every horizon, and publishes pointwise (mean +/- 1.96 SE of the same fixed-b-scaled replicates) and simultaneous bands. Multi-step / joint Sequence overlays use the same construction on the unfolded sequential engine: each replicate resamples lag-aligned outcome-time tuples, refits every unfolded mechanism of every horizon and recomputes root-node means (observation pairs refit the nuisance on the resampled outcome-time tuples; selected-AIPW folds are contiguous time blocks keyed by each tuple's source position; observation-adjusted Sequences whose unfolded design carries the outcome at a nonzero lag are refused) and publishes pointwise and simultaneous bands; the 1.8 raw-series observation replicate (31-81% coverage) is gone. Measured 400-replicate coverage at nominal 0.95: curves, Set/Shift, horizon-dependent I(h) 0.925-0.970 (not a registry value); Selected observation pair 0.948-0.983 (not a registry value); two-step Sequence 0.940-0.963 (not a registry value); Selected two-step Sequence 0.953-0.975 (not a registry value) (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_response_calibration.rs). The selected-AIPW outcome nuisance conditions on the declared observation set and on every downstream design column (each horizon's lag-aligned treatment and adjustment columns; the outcome mechanism's parents for Sequence overlays), so the pseudo-outcome regression is orthogonal to the estimated selection probability; with the declared set alone the nuisance omitted the second treatment lag and the iid Selected Sequence band covered 0.983 (not a registry value). Repeated-sampling coverage (400 replicates, n=160, pointwise per cell and simultaneous per surface) is gated on iid and AR(1) rho=0.5 residuals and on both the dose curve (0.945-0.948 / 0.948 (not a registry value)) and the shift response (0.943 / 0.948 (not a registry value); 0.910 / 0.912 (not a registry value) before the kernel-bias factor; about ten effective rows, short-series-warned) under an AR(1) phi=0.9 treatment; AR(1) rho=0.9 residuals (0.887-0.943 pointwise, 0.877 (not a registry value) simultaneous at n=160; 0.900-0.922 / 0.895 (not a registry value) at n=400; 0.902-0.943 / 0.905 (not a registry value) at n=1000) remain an unasserted boundary record, disclosed on every band as response.temporal.block.persistence_boundary: the persistent part is 15% of the residual and its long-run ratio is not estimable at these n: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_response_calibration.rs. |

| `PulseEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/temporal_dose_horizon`) | none | Dispatches through execute_temporal to TemporalLinearAdjustment directly, not by slicing a ResponseCurve surface; agrees numerically with surface[active]-surface[control] at the pulse lag and horizon on temporal_dose_horizon via shared adjustment machinery. Identification (Z@-1, temporal.backdoor.unfolded) and the structural contrast 2 are additionally pinned on temporal_confounded_pulse. Prepared TemporalEffect identification reuse is required. Frequentist uncertainty: se_bootstrap is a circular-block bootstrap over consecutive lag-aligned rows (each replicate refits the design; block at least max(history + horizon, ⌈n^{1/3}⌉) rows, lengthened to ⌈b_PW·n^{1/6}⌉, at most n/3, when the treatment influence or any normal-equation score of the regression (residuals included) is persistently dependent), scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor; se_analytic is NaN because the iid OLS SE ignores serial dependence and a Newey–West SE at that bandwidth under-covered at nominal 0.90 during 1.9 development (that run was not retained); with zero replicates no SE is published. Assumes the estimating score's serial dependence is short relative to the series: below 45 score effective rows (the smaller of the lag-1 and block-length readings; threshold measured in docs/short-series-thresholds.md) the result warns estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series. With an AR(1) treatment as well as AR(1) residuals the interval measured 0.778–0.870 (not a registry value) at nominal 0.90 where the series is short for that memory (ρ ≥ 0.9 up to n = 160, docs/short-series-thresholds.md), warned on at least 94% of replicates; the gate's boundary cell at ρ = 0.9, n = 60 measured 0.810 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.frequentist.circular_block_se.l90.temporal_dag_pulse_ar1_treatment_rho09_n60_short_series_boundary`) (v19_temporal_frequentist::temporal_dag_pulse_ar1_treatment_rho09_n60_short_series_boundary); with an MA(3) treatment and AR(1) ρ = 0.9 residuals it covers nominally at n = 60 and 160 (gated). Coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_frequentist.rs (iid and AR(1) residuals, horizons 1 and 2). When unfolding cannot certify because an ancestor of the treatment lies on a lagged cycle (for example an autoregressive treatment edge t[t-1] -> t[t], as PCMCI commonly discovers), the single-step pulse is identified by parent adjustment: Z = pa(T[t]), its lagged and contemporaneous parents, derivation rule temporal.parent_adjustment. Its premises are recorded as identification assumptions and enter the identification-product identity: causal sufficiency (the accepted TemporalDag carries no latent or bidirected structure) and every parent an oriented edge observed at its lag; a parent beyond max_history_lag refuses. The fit is the same estimator on the declared set. Multi-step Sustained, TemporalCpdag/TemporalPag completions and graph-posterior atoms are identified by unfolding alone. Known truth and refusals: crates/antecedent/tests/temporal_parent_adjustment.rs; coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v110_calibration_temporal.rs::pulse_effect_temporal_dag_autoregressive_parent_adjustment_nominal_coverage. |

Its premises are recorded as identification assumptions and enter the identification-product identity: causal sufficiency (the accepted TemporalDag carries no latent or bidirected structure) and every parent an oriented edge observed at its lag; a parent beyond max_history_lag refuses. | `PulseEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/temporal_pulse`) | none | BayesianTemporalGcomp on the lag-aligned design; fixture n=400 pins the effect mean. Same-design and mapped prior transfer may ride this cell via conformance/bayesian/temporal_prior_transfer (named source/target + PriorCatalog.filter_compatible); incompatible catalogs fail closed. Not a matrix axis. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Transfer binds lag-aware coefficient names (coef_<var>@lag<k>): identical-subspace or effect-functional transfer across a different lag, window, or covariate layout, or from an artifact without lag-aware names, fails closed unless an explicit NamedParameters mapping is declared. When unfolding cannot certify because an ancestor of the treatment lies on a lagged cycle (for example an autoregressive treatment edge t[t-1] -> t[t], as PCMCI commonly discovers), the single-step pulse is identified by parent adjustment: Z = pa(T[t]), its lagged and contemporaneous parents, derivation rule temporal.parent_adjustment. Its premises are recorded as identification assumptions and enter the identification-product identity: causal sufficiency (the accepted TemporalDag carries no latent or bidirected structure) and every parent an oriented edge observed at its lag; a parent beyond max_history_lag refuses. The fit is the same estimator on the declared set. Multi-step Sustained, TemporalCpdag/TemporalPag completions and graph-posterior atoms are identified by unfolding alone. Known truth and refusals: crates/antecedent/tests/temporal_parent_adjustment.rs. |

Full prior sensitivity perturbs the prior in force (variance-multiplier grid around a staged or transferred prior, alpha grid for a prior-bank compose, isotropic scale grid only when no prior was supplied); the prior PPC simulates from that prior and the posterior PPC adds a lag-1 residual-autocorrelation (serial) discrepancy. Under full, bootstrap.ci_coverage refits the least-squares contrast on circular blocks of lag-aligned rows as a stand-in for the posterior mean and is not applicable when prior shrinkage separates the two by more than 0.25 posterior SD. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. When unfolding cannot certify because an ancestor of the treatment lies on a lagged cycle (for example an autoregressive treatment edge t[t-1] -> t[t], as PCMCI commonly discovers), the single-step pulse is identified by parent adjustment: Z = pa(T[t]), its lagged and contemporaneous parents, derivation rule temporal.parent_adjustment. Its premises are recorded as identification assumptions and enter the identification-product identity: causal sufficiency (the accepted TemporalDag carries no latent or bidirected structure) and every parent an oriented edge observed at its lag; a parent beyond max_history_lag refuses. The fit is the same estimator on the declared set. Multi-step Sustained, TemporalCpdag/TemporalPag completions and graph-posterior atoms are identified by unfolding alone. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. The prior PPC simulates from the prior in force. When unfolding cannot certify because an ancestor of the treatment lies on a lagged cycle (for example an autoregressive treatment edge t[t-1] -> t[t], as PCMCI commonly discovers), the single-step pulse is identified by parent adjustment: Z = pa(T[t]), its lagged and contemporaneous parents, derivation rule temporal.parent_adjustment. Its premises are recorded as identification assumptions and enter the identification-product identity: causal sufficiency (the accepted TemporalDag carries no latent or bidirected structure) and every parent an oriented edge observed at its lag; a parent beyond max_history_lag refuses. The fit is the same estimator on the declared set. Multi-step Sustained, TemporalCpdag/TemporalPag completions and graph-posterior atoms are identified by unfolding alone. Known truth and refusals: crates/antecedent/tests/temporal_parent_adjustment.rs. |
| `PulseEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `accepted` | `Bayesian` | `full` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/temporal_pulse`) | discovery oracles when the accepted graph is discovered: `discovery.pcmci` | Full adds PPC / prior-sensitivity; the fixture pins the Bayesian pulse mean, not those checks. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Full prior sensitivity perturbs the prior in force (variance-multiplier grid around a staged or transferred prior, alpha grid for a prior-bank compose, isotropic scale grid only when no prior was supplied); the prior PPC simulates from that prior and the posterior PPC adds a lag-1 residual-autocorrelation (serial) discrepancy. Under full, bootstrap.ci_coverage refits the least-squares contrast on circular blocks of lag-aligned rows as a stand-in for the posterior mean and is not applicable when prior shrinkage separates the two by more than 0.25 posterior SD. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. When unfolding cannot certify because an ancestor of the treatment lies on a lagged cycle (for example an autoregressive treatment edge t[t-1] -> t[t], as PCMCI commonly discovers), the single-step pulse is identified by parent adjustment: Z = pa(T[t]), its lagged and contemporaneous parents, derivation rule temporal.parent_adjustment. Its premises are recorded as identification assumptions and enter the identification-product identity: causal sufficiency (the accepted TemporalDag carries no latent or bidirected structure) and every parent an oriented edge observed at its lag; a parent beyond max_history_lag refuses. The fit is the same estimator on the declared set. Multi-step Sustained, TemporalCpdag/TemporalPag completions and graph-posterior atoms are identified by unfolding alone. Known truth and refusals: crates/antecedent/tests/temporal_parent_adjustment.rs. |
| `SustainedEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/temporal_dose_horizon`) | none | Single-step sustained uses the existing identified temporal adjustment design and its known-truth fixture. Multi-step windows additionally use temporal.sequential.gcomp: every treatment offset is intervened on, on an identified unfolded linear-additive Dag, AllObserved complete rows, no split. Extra evidence: conformance/bayesian/response_surfaces and conformance/bayesian/release_12_moments. Multi-step Frequentist uncertainty resamples circular blocks of consecutive lag-aligned rows shared across mechanisms (block at least max(max lag + 1, ⌈n^{1/3}⌉) rows, lengthened to ⌈b_PW·n^{1/6}⌉, at most n/3, when the contrast influence or any mechanism's normal-equation score (residuals included) is persistently dependent), and the replicate SD is scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor. It is gated under AR(1) on one TemporalDag (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_frequentist.rs: ρ = 0.5 at n = 160, ρ = 0.9 at n = 400) and on DBN and class mixtures (v19_calibration.rs, v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs); below 40 score effective rows the multi-step result warns estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series (an AR(1) treatment at ρ = 0.95, n = 60 measured 0.840: v19_temporal_frequentist::temporal_dag_multistep_sustained_ar1_treatment_rho095_n60_short_series_boundary, a boundary record). Frequentist uncertainty: se_bootstrap is a circular-block bootstrap over consecutive lag-aligned rows (each replicate refits the design; block at least max(history + horizon, ⌈n^{1/3}⌉) rows, lengthened to ⌈b_PW·n^{1/6}⌉, at most n/3, when the treatment influence or any normal-equation score of the regression (residuals included) is persistently dependent), scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor; se_analytic is NaN because the iid OLS SE ignores serial dependence and a Newey–West SE at that bandwidth under-covered at nominal 0.90 during 1.9 development (that run was not retained); with zero replicates no SE is published. Assumes the estimating score's serial dependence is short relative to the series: below 45 score effective rows (the smaller of the lag-1 and block-length readings; threshold measured in docs/short-series-thresholds.md) the result warns estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series. With an AR(1) treatment as well as AR(1) residuals the interval measured 0.778–0.870 (not a registry value) at nominal 0.90 where the series is short for that memory (ρ ≥ 0.9 up to n = 160, docs/short-series-thresholds.md), warned on at least 94% of replicates; the gate's boundary cell at ρ = 0.9, n = 60 measured 0.810 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.frequentist.circular_block_se.l90.temporal_dag_pulse_ar1_treatment_rho09_n60_short_series_boundary`) (v19_temporal_frequentist::temporal_dag_pulse_ar1_treatment_rho09_n60_short_series_boundary); with an MA(3) treatment and AR(1) ρ = 0.9 residuals it covers nominally at n = 60 and 160 (gated). Coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_frequentist.rs (iid and AR(1) residuals, horizons 1 and 2). |

| `SustainedEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/temporal_sustained`) | none | Single-step sustained uses the existing identified temporal adjustment design and its known-truth fixture. Multi-step windows additionally use temporal.sequential.gcomp: every treatment offset is intervened on, on an identified unfolded linear-additive Dag, AllObserved complete rows, no split. Bayesian conjugate/Laplace fit each stationary Gaussian mechanism once on its complete observed rows and reuse its coefficient draws across time copies. HMC composition requires derived-contrast diagnostics and is refused. Extra evidence: conformance/bayesian/response_surfaces and conformance/bayesian/release_12_moments. Same-design and mapped prior transfer may ride the single-step cell via conformance/bayesian/temporal_prior_transfer; incompatible catalogs fail closed. Multi-step windows keep isotropic per-mechanism priors. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory; multi-step windows use each stationary mechanism's contrast gradient), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Transfer binds lag-aware coefficient names (coef_<var>@lag<k>): identical-subspace or effect-functional transfer across a different lag, window, or covariate layout, or from an artifact without lag-aware names, fails closed unless an explicit NamedParameters mapping is declared. |

The prior PPC simulates from the prior in force. The cheap suite does not run bootstrap.ci_coverage; under full, multi-step windows check it against the least-squares contrast as a stand-in for the tempered posterior mean (refute.bootstrap.ci_coverage.least_squares_stand_in), and it is NotApplicable under an informative coefficient prior. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. |

Full prior sensitivity perturbs the prior in force (variance-multiplier grid around a staged or transferred prior, alpha grid for a prior-bank compose, isotropic scale grid only when no prior was supplied, which is always the case for multi-step windows because they refuse transfer); the prior PPC simulates from that prior and the posterior PPC adds a lag-1 residual-autocorrelation (serial) discrepancy. On multi-step windows bootstrap.ci_coverage resamples circular blocks of lag-aligned rows sized by the Frequentist contrast interval's rule (dependence_block_length over the contrast influence and every mechanism's normal-equation scores) and refits the least-squares contrast as a stand-in for the posterior mean: with each likelihood tempered by 1/kappa under the isotropic prior of scale s, the posterior mean is the ridge fit (X'X + kappa/s^2 I)^-1 X'y, least squares only up to O(kappa/(n s^2)) shrinkage (the check is not applicable when that shrinkage moves the posterior mean more than 0.25 posterior SD from least squares); the refute.bootstrap.ci_coverage.least_squares_stand_in diagnostic discloses this. bootstrap.ci_coverage is NotApplicable under an informative coefficient prior. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. |
