| `InterventionalDistribution` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/functional_validation`) | component oracles: `estimate.identify.backdoor` (identifier:general.id), `estimate.identify.general_id` (identifier:general.id) | Finite discrete CPT evaluator on a supplied or accepted Dag. Evidence: conformance/estimate/functional_validation through the staged handle (functional_validation_staged_known_truth); conformance/estimate/interventional_distribution is pinned by the crate's end_to_end_interventional_distribution test. cheap/full are licensed separately with per-stratum normalization and full-table total-variation stability; descriptive mean TV threshold 0.1 is not a calibrated test. Analysis masks are honored. No scalar-mean surrogate or continuous density claim. Every atom probability publishes a 95% logit-scale delta-method interval from its own bootstrap SE, expit(logit p̂ ± z·se/(p̂(1 − p̂))), which always lies inside [0, 1]; a binary {0, 1} outcome's interventional mean uses the Y = 1 atom's interval (a symmetric mean ± z·se can leave [0, 1] near the boundary). A plug-in probability of exactly 0 or 1, a failed bootstrap or zero bootstrap spread publishes no interval and names the atom in the estimate.distribution.interval_unavailable warning. 400-replicate 95% coverage of P(Y = 1 \| do(t)) (n = 600, the Study default of 199 bootstrap replicates): 0.935 at 0.035, 0.950 at 0.465, 0.945 at 0.965 (not a registry value) (coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_calibration.rs::interventional_distribution_frequentist_near_zero_nominal_95_coverage, ::interventional_distribution_frequentist_interior_nominal_95_coverage, ::interventional_distribution_frequentist_near_one_nominal_95_coverage, calibration gate). |
| `TemporalMediationEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | frozen_external_oracle (`conformance/estimate/temporal_mediation_grid`) | cell output compared with `conformance/estimate/temporal_mediation_grid`; component oracles: `estimate.temporal_mediation.external_parity` (estimator:temporal.mediation) | One mediator, lag-one treatment, linear additive mechanisms without treatment-mediator interaction; the mediator (M ~ T + S), outcome (Y ~ T + M + S) and reduced-form (Y ~ T + S) regressions and the refuters share one adjustment set per horizon, S(h) = I(h) (the T→Y back-door set) plus every parent of M and Y that is not a treatment or mediator descendant, certified on the unfolded DAG as a T→M, joint (T, M)→Y and T→Y back-door set; S(h) is recorded in the temporal_mediation.adjustment_sets assumption and the mediation_grid slice adjustment. A horizon whose mediator-outcome confounding only a treatment descendant would block is refused (NotCertified). Treatment-induced intermediate covariates require a larger path model. Natural effects use the explicit no-interaction alias. Frequentist uncertainty: iid analytic SEs are NaN for Total, Direct and Mediated (TemporalMediationEstimator::allow_iid_sobel_se opts back in); requested replicates run one circular-block bootstrap over consecutive lag-aligned rows (block at least max(deepest design lag + 1, ⌈n^{1/3}⌉) rows, lengthened to ⌈b_PW·n^{1/6}⌉, at most n/3, when a Total, Direct or Mediated influence or any mechanism's normal-equation score (residuals included) is persistently dependent: dependence_block_length) that refits all three mechanism regressions, so the three SEs share replicates; they are scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor and published per contrast in mediation_grid (FrequentistBlockBootstrap), with the requested contrast as se_bootstrap. With zero replicates no SE is published. Below 40 score effective rows (the smallest, over the three contrasts' persistence probes — each mechanism residual times the centred treatment or mediator — of the lag-1 and block-length readings; threshold measured in docs/short-series-thresholds.md) the result warns estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series: with an AR(1) treatment as well as AR(1) residuals Total and Direct measured 0.792–0.880 (not a registry value) where the series is short for that memory (docs/short-series-thresholds.md; the gate's boundary cell at ρ = 0.9, n = 60 measured 0.807 / 0.818 (not a registry value), v19_temporal_frequentist::temporal_dag_mediation_ar1_treatment_rho09_n60_short_series_boundary), while the MA(3)-treatment design covers nominally at ρ = 0.9, n = 60 and 160 (gated). Coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_frequentist.rs, including a mediator-outcome-confounded DGP. No general mediation or calibrated refuter claim. cheap/full are licensed separately with placebo-mediator, RCC, and contiguous-window diagnostics. |

| `AverageEffect` | `Dag` | `accepted` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/shared_functional_ate`) | component oracles: `estimate.identify.backdoor` (identifier:backdoor.adjustment); discovery oracles when the accepted graph is discovered: `discovery.lingam` | Accepted Dag uses the same direct structural-ATE posterior-mean check as explicit Dag and additionally cross-checks frequentist linear adjustment. Backend posteriors are pinned separately. bayesian.gcomp declares a Gaussian identity-link outcome regression linear in the treatment and each adjustment column with no treatment × covariate interaction (assumption bayesian.gcomp.linear_gaussian_outcome); its credible interval is for that model's treatment contrast. `Bayesian(likelihood=logit\|probit\|poisson)` fits a Bernoulli or Poisson GLM on this cell instead (the assumption names that family) and averages the inverse-link contrast over the rows; every other Bayesian cell, the conjugate backend and a transferred prior refuse a non-Gaussian likelihood with `likelihood_not_supported`, and a Gaussian fit to a 0/1 or nonnegative-integer outcome reports `estimate.bayesian.gaussian_likelihood_discrete_outcome`. 400-replicate 90% coverage 0.890 (record `cov.average_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_gcomp_misspecification_probe.linear_outcome`) on a linear law; R-17 misspecification probes measured 0.705 (not a registry value) with an omitted z² term (bias 0.154) and 0.215 (not a registry value) with an omitted t·z interaction (bias 0.282) (v19_static_calibration::bayesian_gcomp_misspecification_probe). |

| `AverageEffect` | `Pag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/pag_ate_envelope_identified`) | component oracles: `estimate.linear_regression` (estimator:linear.adjustment.ate) | conformance/estimate/pag_ate_envelope_identified: seven MAG completions of the supplied PAG, six identified by generalized adjustment with visible edges out of the treatment at two different effects (adjust {z}: direct; adjust {}: total through z -> m -> y) and one unidentified (no arrowhead into the treatment; mass 1/7, reported, never mixed in; status GraphDependent). The point is the equal-weight mixture over identified completions (0.37443451148801365 on the fixture); the SE is the joint influence-function SE of that mixture on shared rows (Frisch-Waugh IF per completion; 0.021664212100486056). Both are pinned against an independent numpy reference (reference.py) on fresh, prepared and refresh paths. The SE is for the mixture functional, not the spread of completion-specific effects. 1.9 calibration on a continuous DGP whose completions disagree: 400-replicate 90% coverage 0.890 (record `cov.average_effect.pag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.static_pag_ate_envelope_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) (v19_static_envelope_calibration::static_pag_ate_envelope_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage; below the band before the residualized-IF fix). A PAG whose treatment edges are invisible in every completion is refused (conformance/estimate/pag_ate_envelope is kept as that negative evidence). estimate.envelope.se_omits_between_atom_variance is emitted only when that SE is non-finite. Prepared handles freeze and reuse the envelope; estimate clicks and same-schema refresh emit exec.identify.cached; a new graph or query requires a new prepare. |

Each identified completion keeps its own conjugate posterior; the published draws are the posterior of the frozen-weight mixture functional, with completions rank-coupled by their influence-function correlation on the shared rows (estimate.envelope.bayesian_mixture_functional). They are not a distribution over completion-specific effects: the earlier identified-atom BMA covered the mixture functional 99.8-100% of the time at nominal 90% and was replaced in 1.9. Static multi-atom Frequentist SE uses joint influence-function covariance on shared rows (frozen completion weights); on the fixture it is pinned at 0.0297936681639348 against an independent numpy reference (cpdag_ate_envelope/reference.py), and 400-replicate 90% coverage on a two-completion DGP whose completions disagree is 0.868 (not a registry value) at 400 replicates and 0.868 (record `cov.average_effect.cpdag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.static_cpdag_ate_envelope_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) at the 2000-replicate recheck (v19_static_envelope_calibration::static_cpdag_ate_envelope_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage; below the band before the residualized-IF fix). estimate.envelope.se_omits_between_atom_variance is emitted only when that SE is non-finite. |

| `PulseEffect` | `TemporalCpdag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/temporal_class_envelope`) | none | Incomplete TemporalCpdag stays TemporalCpdag, including a fully oriented supplied graph. Completions are TemporalDag MEC members; each is identified with temporal.backdoor.unfolded and estimated with temporal.linear.adjustment, then mass-weighted. Completing the graph yourself is still the TemporalDag coordinate. Prepared clicks reuse the frozen envelope and emit exec.identify.cached. Requested replicates share one circular-block resample across completions with frozen weights; unidentified mass is not mixed into the SE. estimate.envelope.se_omits_between_atom_variance is emitted only when that SE is non-finite. Frequentist uncertainty (when replicates are requested) is a shared circular-block bootstrap over lag-aligned series times: blocks of consecutive times are resampled over the window where every completion's maximal lag window is available, each completion's lag-aligned design keeps its rows' original lag windows (no row pairs an outcome with lags from an unrelated block), every contributing completion is refit on the same resampled times, and the replicate SD is scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor. Block length is max(span, ⌈m^{1/3}⌉) over the m shared times (span = the unfolded history + horizon window), lengthened to ⌈b_PW·m^{1/6}⌉ (the Politis-White length at the fixed-b testing rate) when any completion's influence, the mixture score, or any fitted regression's normal-equation score (residuals included) is persistently dependent; estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series warns when the score effective rows (the smallest over every atom's and the mixture's score of the lag-1 and block-length readings) fall below 155 (docs/short-series-thresholds.md). That threshold covers a bias the block bootstrap cannot remove: at ρ ≥ 0.9 an atom that omits a persistent confounder is biased in finite samples (in the docs/short-series-thresholds.md sweep, v19_short_series_measurement, TemporalCpdag Pulse with two completions measured 0.685–0.905 (not a registry value) at ρ ≥ 0.9, n ≤ 400 and DBN multi-step Sustained 0.859–0.862 (not a registry value) at ρ = 0.95, n ≤ 60: a warned boundary). The scalar identified set carries identified_set_interval, an Imbens-Manski interval with per-completion endpoints from the same replicates that covers the true effect with asymptotic probability at least 90% whenever it is one retained identified completion's effect (conservative while completions nearly agree relative to their SDs; flagged truncated, with a warning, when the completion enumeration or its equivalence audit is capped). Coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_calibration.rs and crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs. |
| `PulseEffect` | `TemporalCpdag` | `accepted` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/temporal_class_envelope`) | discovery oracles when the accepted graph is discovered: `discovery.pcmci_plus` | Accepted TemporalCpdag uses the same mass-weighted temporal-backdoor envelope as explicit. Prepared clicks reuse the frozen envelope. Requested replicates share one circular-block resample across completions with frozen weights; unidentified mass is not mixed into the SE. estimate.envelope.se_omits_between_atom_variance is emitted only when that SE is non-finite. Frequentist uncertainty (when replicates are requested) is a shared circular-block bootstrap over lag-aligned series times: blocks of consecutive times are resampled over the window where every completion's maximal lag window is available, each completion's lag-aligned design keeps its rows' original lag windows (no row pairs an outcome with lags from an unrelated block), every contributing completion is refit on the same resampled times, and the replicate SD is scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor. Block length is max(span, ⌈m^{1/3}⌉) over the m shared times (span = the unfolded history + horizon window), lengthened to ⌈b_PW·m^{1/6}⌉ (the Politis-White length at the fixed-b testing rate) when any completion's influence, the mixture score, or any fitted regression's normal-equation score (residuals included) is persistently dependent; estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series warns when the score effective rows (the smallest over every atom's and the mixture's score of the lag-1 and block-length readings) fall below 155 (docs/short-series-thresholds.md). That threshold covers a bias the block bootstrap cannot remove: at ρ ≥ 0.9 an atom that omits a persistent confounder is biased in finite samples (in the docs/short-series-thresholds.md sweep, v19_short_series_measurement, TemporalCpdag Pulse with two completions measured 0.685–0.905 (not a registry value) at ρ ≥ 0.9, n ≤ 400 and DBN multi-step Sustained 0.859–0.862 (not a registry value) at ρ = 0.95, n ≤ 60: a warned boundary). The scalar identified set carries identified_set_interval, an Imbens-Manski interval with per-completion endpoints from the same replicates that covers the true effect with asymptotic probability at least 90% whenever it is one retained identified completion's effect (conservative while completions nearly agree relative to their SDs; flagged truncated, with a warning, when the completion enumeration or its equivalence audit is capped). Coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_calibration.rs and crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs. |

| `PulseEffect` | `TemporalPag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/temporal_class_envelope`) | none | Incomplete TemporalPag stays TemporalPag, including a fully oriented supplied graph. Circle marks refine to directed or bidirected MAG members; each is identified with temporal MAG adjustment after a visibility check, then mass-weighted. Finite-window equivalence is audited; a capped audit cannot confer class-wide point identification. Tail-tail selection edges refuse. Completing the graph yourself is still the TemporalDag coordinate. Prepared clicks reuse the frozen envelope. Requested replicates share one circular-block resample across completions with frozen weights; unidentified mass is not mixed into the SE. estimate.envelope.se_omits_between_atom_variance is emitted only when that SE is non-finite. Positive multi-completion evidence: temporal_class_envelope/identified_pag.json has seven stationary MAG completions, six identified at two different lag-1 effects (adjust z@-1: 1.9618; adjust nothing: 2.7205) and one unidentified (mass 1/7). The mixture 2.214723444204995 is pinned against a numpy reference for Pulse and single-step Sustained, fresh and prepared, explicit and accepted, none/cheap/full; with 32 replicates the shared circular-block SE is finite and positive and estimate.temporal_class.frequentist.shared_block is emitted. Repeated-sampling coverage of a multi-completion TemporalPag with the same structure and a stochastic linear-Gaussian law is gated in crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs (Pulse, single- and multi-step Sustained, AR(1) variants; AR(1) rho = 0.9, n = 400 is a warned short_series boundary, measured and not gated). Frequentist uncertainty (when replicates are requested) is a shared circular-block bootstrap over lag-aligned series times: blocks of consecutive times are resampled over the window where every completion's maximal lag window is available, each completion's lag-aligned design keeps its rows' original lag windows (no row pairs an outcome with lags from an unrelated block), every contributing completion is refit on the same resampled times, and the replicate SD is scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor. Repeated-sampling coverage of a multi-completion TemporalPag with the same structure and a stochastic linear-Gaussian law is gated in crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs (Pulse, single- and multi-step Sustained, AR(1) variants; AR(1) rho = 0.9, n = 400 is a warned short_series boundary, measured and not gated). Frequentist uncertainty (when replicates are requested) is a shared circular-block bootstrap over lag-aligned series times: blocks of consecutive times are resampled over the window where every completion's maximal lag window is available, each completion's lag-aligned design keeps its rows' original lag windows (no row pairs an outcome with lags from an unrelated block), every contributing completion is refit on the same resampled times, and the replicate SD is scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor. Block length is max(span, ⌈m^{1/3}⌉) over the m shared times (span = the unfolded history + horizon window), lengthened to ⌈b_PW·m^{1/6}⌉ (the Politis-White length at the fixed-b testing rate) when any completion's influence, the mixture score, or any fitted regression's normal-equation score (residuals included) is persistently dependent; estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series warns when the score effective rows (the smallest over every atom's and the mixture's score of the lag-1 and block-length readings) fall below 155 (docs/short-series-thresholds.md). That threshold covers a bias the block bootstrap cannot remove: at ρ ≥ 0.9 an atom that omits a persistent confounder is biased in finite samples (in the docs/short-series-thresholds.md sweep, v19_short_series_measurement, TemporalCpdag Pulse with two completions measured 0.685–0.905 (not a registry value) at ρ ≥ 0.9, n ≤ 400 and DBN multi-step Sustained 0.859–0.862 (not a registry value) at ρ = 0.95, n ≤ 60: a warned boundary). The scalar identified set carries identified_set_interval, an Imbens-Manski interval with per-completion endpoints from the same replicates that covers the true effect with asymptotic probability at least 90% whenever it is one retained identified completion's effect (conservative while completions nearly agree relative to their SDs; flagged truncated, with a warning, when the completion enumeration or its equivalence audit is capped). Coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_calibration.rs and crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs. |

That threshold covers a bias the block bootstrap cannot remove: at ρ ≥ 0.9 an atom that omits a persistent confounder is biased in finite samples (in the docs/short-series-thresholds.md sweep, v19_short_series_measurement, TemporalCpdag Pulse with two completions measured 0.685–0.905 (not a registry value) at ρ ≥ 0.9, n ≤ 400 and DBN multi-step Sustained 0.859–0.862 (not a registry value) at ρ = 0.95, n ≤ 60: a warned boundary). The scalar identified set carries identified_set_interval, an Imbens-Manski interval with per-completion endpoints from the same replicates that covers the true effect with asymptotic probability at least 90% whenever it is one retained identified completion's effect (conservative while completions nearly agree relative to their SDs; flagged truncated, with a warning, when the completion enumeration or its equivalence audit is capped). Coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_calibration.rs and crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs. |

Requested replicates share one circular-block resample across completions with frozen weights; unidentified mass is not mixed into the SE. estimate.envelope.se_omits_between_atom_variance is emitted only when that SE is non-finite. | `PulseEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Frozen structural mixture pins the lag-one Pulse effect at 0.9; a valid autoregressive atom reaches the finite-history certification boundary, so its 0.3 weight remains unidentified and priors do not upgrade ID. The staged prepared result equals fresh and consumes cached per-atom identification. cheap/full are licensed separately: effect refuters run on every contributing atom against that atom's own posterior mean and mix by posterior mass, plus PPC on cheap; full adds prior-sensitivity aggregating contributing atoms. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |
| `SustainedEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Frozen structural mixture pins single-step Sustained and two-step Sustained [-2, -1] at 0.9; a valid autoregressive atom reaches the finite-history certification boundary, so its 0.3 weight remains unidentified and priors do not upgrade ID. Multi-step uses sequential g-computation with no last-step collapse. The staged prepared result equals fresh and consumes cached per-atom identification. Single-step cheap/full retain temporal refuters. Multi-step cheap/full refit the composed sequential effect per atom; Bayesian PPC checks stationary mechanisms and full prior sensitivity refits the whole effect. Evidence: crates/antecedent/tests/dbn_sustained_validation.rs. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory; multi-step windows use each stationary mechanism's contrast gradient), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |

Policy pins: crates/antecedent/tests/manufacturing_temporal.rs::dbn_posterior_graph_posterior_numeric_pins. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |

| `PathSpecificEffect` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `cheap` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/functional_validation`) | none | Discrete natural path functional on a supplied or accepted Dag; selected path and cross-world identification assumptions remain frozen. Cheap refits twenty 80-percent subsets. These are path-contrast stability diagnostics, not ATE refuters or calibrated causal-validity tests; sparse CPT failures refuse. Evidence: functional_validation and docs/v1.2-evidence.md. Since 1.9 a directed treatment–outcome path outside the selected set is handled by the edge g-formula (Avin, Shpitser & Pearl 2005; Shpitser 2013): each P(v \| pa(v)) over the outcome's ancestors takes the treatment at the active level on edges that start a selected path and at the control level elsewhere, minus the all-control g-formula; when every path is selected the effect is the total effect and general ID runs on the surgical graph. A recanting witness (a treatment child that starts both a selected and an unselected directed path, which is exactly the Avin–Shpitser–Pearl criterion; a node shared by paths that leave the treatment through different children is not one) and a truncated path enumeration are refused as not identified. Known truth: conformance/estimate/path_specific_edge_gformula (path effect 0.12, total 0.356; shared-descendant case path effect 0.125, total 0.0625; path_specific_edge_gformula_pins). 400-replicate 90% coverage: chain 0.887 (record `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.frequentist.bootstrap_se.l90.path_specific_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) (Frequentist bootstrap) and 0.895 (record `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.path_specific_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`) (Bayesian); two-path confounded law 0.882 (record `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.frequentist.bootstrap_se.l90.path_specific_two_path_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.882 (record `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.path_specific_two_path_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`) (v19_static_calibration::path_specific_*). |

| `InterventionalDistribution` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `cheap` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/functional_validation`) | component oracles: `estimate.identify.backdoor` (identifier:general.id), `estimate.identify.general_id` (identifier:general.id) | Finite discrete CPT evaluator on a supplied or accepted Dag. Cheap checks per-stratum normalization and full-table total-variation stability over twenty 80-percent subsets. Descriptive mean TV threshold 0.1 is not a calibrated test. Analysis masks are honored. No scalar-mean surrogate or continuous density claim. Every atom probability publishes a 95% logit-scale delta-method interval from its own bootstrap SE, expit(logit p̂ ± z·se/(p̂(1 − p̂))), which always lies inside [0, 1]; a binary {0, 1} outcome's interventional mean uses the Y = 1 atom's interval (a symmetric mean ± z·se can leave [0, 1] near the boundary). A plug-in probability of exactly 0 or 1, a failed bootstrap or zero bootstrap spread publishes no interval and names the atom in the estimate.distribution.interval_unavailable warning. 400-replicate 95% coverage of P(Y = 1 \| do(t)) (n = 600, the Study default of 199 bootstrap replicates): 0.935 at 0.035, 0.950 at 0.465, 0.945 at 0.965 (not a registry value) (v19_static_calibration::interventional_distribution_frequentist_*). |

Every atom probability publishes a 95% logit-scale delta-method interval from its own bootstrap SE, expit(logit p̂ ± z·se/(p̂(1 − p̂))), which always lies inside [0, 1]; a binary {0, 1} outcome's interventional mean uses the Y = 1 atom's interval (a symmetric mean ± z·se can leave [0, 1] near the boundary). A plug-in probability of exactly 0 or 1, a failed bootstrap or zero bootstrap spread publishes no interval and names the atom in the estimate.distribution.interval_unavailable warning. 400-replicate 95% coverage of P(Y = 1 \| do(t)) (n = 600, the Study default of 199 bootstrap replicates): 0.935 at 0.035, 0.950 at 0.465, 0.945 at 0.965 (not a registry value) (v19_static_calibration::interventional_distribution_frequentist_*). |

| `ConditionalEffect` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/conditional_effect`) | component oracles: `estimate.identify.backdoor` (identifier:backdoor.adjustment) | Gaussian linear interaction regression averaged over one modifier; since 1.9 each posterior draw adds Bayesian-bootstrap uncertainty in the modifier mean (holding it fixed covered 86.6% at nominal 90% over 2000 replicates). AllObserved. 400-replicate 90% coverage 0.890 (record `cov.conditional_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.conditional_effect_dag_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`) (v19_static_envelope_calibration::conditional_effect_dag_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage). Bayesian mode fits an actual posterior with centered treatment-by-modifier interaction; no nonlinear link or conditional forest. Independent posterior moments at prior scales 0.1 and 10, artifact roundtrips, structural effect pin. cheap/full are licensed separately: cheap is binary propensity overlap or continuous conditional residual-support assessment, plus E-value and PPC; full adds prior sensitivity. |

| `ConditionalEffect` | `Cpdag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/cpdag_ate_envelope`) | component oracles: `estimate.conditional` (estimator:conditional.linear.adjustment) | Same MEC envelope as the Cpdag ATE cell. The modifier must be pre-treatment and the augmented conditioning set must block backdoor paths. The mediator completion retains unverified mass; the supported conditional effect is 0.40 and status is GraphDependent. Runtime class stays Cpdag. Admg stays refused. Prepared clicks reuse the frozen envelope and emit exec.identify.cached. Estimator is conditional.linear.adjustment. none is the point estimate. Static multi-atom Frequentist SE uses joint influence-function covariance on shared rows (frozen completion weights); the conditional influence function includes the sampling variance of the modifier mean. 400-replicate 90% coverage with a separate outcome-only modifier and disagreeing completions: 0.873 (not a registry value) at 400 replicates and 0.870 (boundary record `cov.conditional_effect.cpdag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.conditional_effect_cpdag_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) at the 2000-replicate recheck (v19_static_envelope_calibration::conditional_effect_cpdag_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage). estimate.envelope.se_omits_between_atom_variance is emitted only when that SE is non-finite. Exceedance and quantile outcome functionals on binary 0/1 arms are computed from cross-fitted AIPW scores (modifier added to the adjustment set), not the interaction model; the result says so (estimate.conditional.crossfit_aipw_scores). |

| `ConditionalEffect` | `Pag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/pag_ate_envelope_identified`) | component oracles: `estimate.conditional` (estimator:conditional.linear.adjustment) | conformance/estimate/pag_ate_envelope_identified: seven MAG completions of the supplied PAG, six identified by generalized adjustment with visible edges out of the treatment at two different effects (adjust {z}: direct; adjust {}: total through z -> m -> y) and one unidentified (no arrowhead into the treatment; mass 1/7, reported, never mixed in; status GraphDependent). Modifier x affects only the outcome, so it is pre-treatment in every completion and the augmented conditioning set blocks back-door paths in the same six. Estimator conditional.linear.adjustment reports b_t + b_tx * mean(x) (the effect averaged over the modifier distribution); its influence function and analytic SE include the sampling variance of the modifier mean. Mixture 0.3743035897503231 and joint-IF SE 0.02138134948402955 are pinned against the numpy reference on fresh, prepared and refresh paths. 400-replicate 90% coverage 0.882 (record `cov.conditional_effect.pag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.conditional_effect_pag_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) (v19_static_envelope_calibration::conditional_effect_pag_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage). Runtime class stays Pag; Admg ConditionalEffect stays refused. estimate.envelope.se_omits_between_atom_variance is emitted only when that SE is non-finite. Prepared handles freeze and reuse the envelope; estimate clicks and same-schema refresh emit exec.identify.cached; a new graph or query requires a new prepare. Exceedance and quantile outcome functionals on binary 0/1 arms are computed from cross-fitted AIPW scores (modifier added to the adjustment set), not the interaction model; the result says so (estimate.conditional.crossfit_aipw_scores). |

Modifier x affects only the outcome and is pre-treatment in every completion. Estimator conditional.bayesian: per-completion conjugate posteriors with a centred treatment-by-modifier interaction, plus Bayesian-bootstrap uncertainty in the modifier mean. Each identified completion keeps its own conjugate posterior; the published draws are the posterior of the frozen-weight mixture functional, with completions rank-coupled by their influence-function correlation on the shared rows (estimate.envelope.bayesian_mixture_functional). They are not a distribution over completion-specific effects: the earlier identified-atom BMA covered the mixture functional 99.8-100% of the time at nominal 90% and was replaced in 1.9. Seeded pins (64 draws): mean 0.37720213089058047, SD 0.02027592757333499. 400-replicate 90% coverage 0.885 (record `cov.conditional_effect.pag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.conditional_effect_pag_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`) (v19_static_envelope_calibration::conditional_effect_pag_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage). | `TemporalMediationEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `cheap` | frozen_external_oracle (`conformance/estimate/temporal_mediation_grid`) | cell output compared with `conformance/estimate/temporal_mediation_grid`; component oracles: `estimate.temporal_mediation.external_parity` (estimator:temporal.mediation) | One mediator, lag-one treatment, linear additive mechanisms without treatment-mediator interaction; the mediator (M ~ T + S), outcome (Y ~ T + M + S) and reduced-form (Y ~ T + S) regressions and the refuters share one adjustment set per horizon, S(h) = I(h) (the T→Y back-door set) plus every parent of M and Y that is not a treatment or mediator descendant, certified on the unfolded DAG as a T→M, joint (T, M)→Y and T→Y back-door set; S(h) is recorded in the temporal_mediation.adjustment_sets assumption and the mediation_grid slice adjustment. A horizon whose mediator-outcome confounding only a treatment descendant would block is refused (NotCertified). Treatment-induced intermediate covariates require a larger path model. Natural effects use the explicit no-interaction alias. Cheap runs placebo-mediator and RCC contrast refits plus empirical binary mediator-range overlap when applicable. Frequentist uncertainty: iid analytic SEs are NaN for Total, Direct and Mediated (TemporalMediationEstimator::allow_iid_sobel_se opts back in); requested replicates run one circular-block bootstrap over consecutive lag-aligned rows (block at least max(deepest design lag + 1, ⌈n^{1/3}⌉) rows, lengthened to ⌈b_PW·n^{1/6}⌉, at most n/3, when a Total, Direct or Mediated influence or any mechanism's normal-equation score (residuals included) is persistently dependent: dependence_block_length) that refits all three mechanism regressions, so the three SEs share replicates; they are scaled by the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor and published per contrast in mediation_grid (FrequentistBlockBootstrap), with the requested contrast as se_bootstrap. With zero replicates no SE is published. Below 40 score effective rows (the smallest, over the three contrasts' persistence probes — each mechanism residual times the centred treatment or mediator — of the lag-1 and block-length readings; threshold measured in docs/short-series-thresholds.md) the result warns estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series: with an AR(1) treatment as well as AR(1) residuals Total and Direct measured 0.792–0.880 (not a registry value) where the series is short for that memory (docs/short-series-thresholds.md; the gate's boundary cell at ρ = 0.9, n = 60 measured 0.807 / 0.818 (not a registry value), v19_temporal_frequentist::temporal_dag_mediation_ar1_treatment_rho09_n60_short_series_boundary), while the MA(3)-treatment design covers nominally at ρ = 0.9, n = 60 and 160 (gated). Coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_frequentist.rs, including a mediator-outcome-confounded DGP. No general mediation or calibrated refuter claim. |

Below 40 score effective rows (the smallest, over the three contrasts' persistence probes — each mechanism residual times the centred treatment or mediator — of the lag-1 and block-length readings; threshold measured in docs/short-series-thresholds.md) the result warns estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series: with an AR(1) treatment as well as AR(1) residuals Total and Direct measured 0.792–0.880 (not a registry value) where the series is short for that memory (docs/short-series-thresholds.md; the gate's boundary cell at ρ = 0.9, n = 60 measured 0.807 / 0.818 (not a registry value), v19_temporal_frequentist::temporal_dag_mediation_ar1_treatment_rho09_n60_short_series_boundary), while the MA(3)-treatment design covers nominally at ρ = 0.9, n = 60 and 160 (gated). Coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_frequentist.rs, including a mediator-outcome-confounded DGP. No general mediation or calibrated refuter claim. |
| `TemporalMediationEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `accepted` | `Frequentist` | `full` | frozen_external_oracle (`conformance/estimate/temporal_mediation_grid`) | cell output compared with `conformance/estimate/temporal_mediation_grid`; component oracles: `estimate.temporal_mediation.external_parity` (estimator:temporal.mediation); discovery oracles when the accepted graph is discovered: `discovery.pcmci` | One mediator, lag-one treatment, linear additive mechanisms without treatment-mediator interaction; the mediator (M ~ T + S), outcome (Y ~ T + M + S) and reduced-form (Y ~ T + S) regressions and the refuters share one adjustment set per horizon, S(h) = I(h) (the T→Y back-door set) plus every parent of M and Y that is not a treatment or mediator descendant, certified on the unfolded DAG as a T→M, joint (T, M)→Y and T→Y back-door set; S(h) is recorded in the temporal_mediation.adjustment_sets assumption and the mediation_grid slice adjustment. A horizon whose mediator-outcome confounding only a treatment descendant would block is refused (NotCertified). Treatment-induced intermediate covariates require a larger path model. With zero replicates no SE is published. Below 40 score effective rows (the smallest, over the three contrasts' persistence probes — each mechanism residual times the centred treatment or mediator — of the lag-1 and block-length readings; threshold measured in docs/short-series-thresholds.md) the result warns estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series: with an AR(1) treatment as well as AR(1) residuals Total and Direct measured 0.792–0.880 (not a registry value) where the series is short for that memory (docs/short-series-thresholds.md; the gate's boundary cell at ρ = 0.9, n = 60 measured 0.807 / 0.818 (not a registry value), v19_temporal_frequentist::temporal_dag_mediation_ar1_treatment_rho09_n60_short_series_boundary), while the MA(3)-treatment design covers nominally at ρ = 0.9, n = 60 and 160 (gated). | `PulseEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Bayesian` | `cheap` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Known-truth DBN graph-mixture effect with identified weights and unidentified mass retained. Applicable temporal refuters run on every contributing atom against that atom's own posterior mean (not the pooled mean) and mix by posterior mass; they do not rediscover graphs. PPC aggregates contributing atoms. Pulse is a single intervention time; multi-step Sustained validation is documented in its own cells. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. The prior PPC simulates from the prior in force. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |
| `PulseEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Bayesian` | `full` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Known-truth DBN graph-mixture effect with identified weights and unidentified mass retained. Applicable temporal refuters run on every contributing atom against that atom's own posterior mean (not the pooled mean) and mix by posterior mass; they do not rediscover graphs. PPC and prior-sensitivity aggregate contributing atoms. Pulse is a single intervention time; multi-step Sustained validation is documented in its own cells. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. Full prior sensitivity perturbs the prior in force (variance-multiplier grid around a staged or transferred prior, alpha grid for a prior-bank compose, isotropic scale grid only when no prior was supplied); the prior PPC simulates from that prior and the posterior PPC adds a lag-1 residual-autocorrelation (serial) discrepancy. Under full, bootstrap.ci_coverage refits the least-squares contrast on circular blocks of lag-aligned rows as a stand-in for the posterior mean and is not applicable when prior shrinkage separates the two by more than 0.25 posterior SD. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |
| `SustainedEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Bayesian` | `cheap` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Known-truth DBN graph-mixture effect with identified weights and unidentified mass retained. Applicable temporal refuters run on every contributing atom against that atom's own posterior mean (not the pooled mean) and mix by posterior mass; they do not rediscover graphs. PPC aggregates contributing atoms. Multi-step cheap/full refit the actual sequential model on each graph atom; the intervention window is preserved. Additional multi-step evidence: crates/antecedent/tests/dbn_sustained_validation.rs pins full-window effects against single-step collapse. Bayesian PPC checks each stationary mechanism separately; full prior sensitivity refits the composed effect. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory; multi-step windows use each stationary mechanism's contrast gradient), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. The prior PPC simulates from the prior in force. The cheap suite does not run bootstrap.ci_coverage; under full, multi-step windows check it against the least-squares contrast as a stand-in for the tempered posterior mean (refute.bootstrap.ci_coverage.least_squares_stand_in), and it is NotApplicable under an informative coefficient prior. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |
| `SustainedEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Bayesian` | `full` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | none | Known-truth DBN graph-mixture effect with identified weights and unidentified mass retained. Applicable temporal refuters run on every contributing atom against that atom's own posterior mean (not the pooled mean) and mix by posterior mass; they do not rediscover graphs. PPC and prior-sensitivity aggregate contributing atoms. Multi-step cheap/full refit the actual sequential model on each graph atom; the intervention window is preserved. Additional multi-step evidence: crates/antecedent/tests/dbn_sustained_validation.rs pins full-window effects against single-step collapse. Bayesian PPC checks each stationary mechanism separately; full prior sensitivity refits the composed effect. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory; multi-step windows use each stationary mechanism's contrast gradient), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. Full prior sensitivity perturbs the prior in force (variance-multiplier grid around a staged or transferred prior, alpha grid for a prior-bank compose, isotropic scale grid only when no prior was supplied, which is always the case for multi-step windows because they refuse transfer); the prior PPC simulates from that prior and the posterior PPC adds a lag-1 residual-autocorrelation (serial) discrepancy. On multi-step windows bootstrap.ci_coverage resamples circular blocks of lag-aligned rows sized by the Frequentist contrast interval's rule (dependence_block_length over the contrast influence and every mechanism's normal-equation scores) and refits the least-squares contrast as a stand-in for the posterior mean: with each likelihood tempered by 1/kappa under the isotropic prior of scale s, the posterior mean is the ridge fit (X'X + kappa/s^2 I)^-1 X'y, least squares only up to O(kappa/(n s^2)) shrinkage (the check is not applicable when that shrinkage moves the posterior mean more than 0.25 posterior SD from least squares); the refute.bootstrap.ci_coverage.least_squares_stand_in diagnostic discloses this. bootstrap.ci_coverage is NotApplicable under an informative coefficient prior. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |

| `ResponseCurve` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/response_surfaces`) | none | Static response.bayesian; Gaussian additive outcome regression, one treatment, complete observations, AllObserved. Known linear surfaces and posterior projection checks. Pointwise credible intervals condition on observed covariates; no simultaneous, nonlinear, derivative, stochastic-policy or graph-posterior response claim. validation cheap/full is not applicable. Credible bands hold the empirical covariate distribution fixed, so they target the sample-covariate curve: 400-replicate 90% pointwise coverage 0.885–0.888 (records `cov.response_curve.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.response_curve_dag_bayesian_pointwise_nominal_90_coverage.a_0`, `cov.response_curve.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.response_curve_dag_bayesian_pointwise_nominal_90_coverage.a_1`) on a linear law; scored against the population curve the same bands cover 0.797–0.870 (not a registry value) (coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_calibration.rs::response_curve_dag_bayesian_pointwise_nominal_90_coverage, calibration gate). |

| `ResponseCurve` | `TemporalDag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/response_surfaces`) | none | response.temporal.bayesian with per-horizon identified designs; Gaussian additive outcome regression, one treatment, AllObserved; complete observations use this backend. Known linear surfaces and posterior projection checks. Pointwise credible intervals and a simultaneous credible band over the grid (response.simultaneous_band.*; max-studentized deviation of posterior draws; across horizons a product of independent per-horizon posteriors, not a joint horizon posterior) condition on the observed covariate and treatment average. Each horizon's likelihood is tempered by 1/kappa_h (a generalized power posterior; kappa_h is the largest autoregressive-prewhitened (AR(1), plus a BIC-selected AR(q ≤ 4)) Newey-West long-run-variance ratio of that horizon's grid-cell level scores, floored at 1; support diagnostic response.temporal_bayesian.tempering), because lag-aligned h-step residuals are MA(h-1) whenever the outcome or treatment is persistent and serially dependent under autocorrelated innovations; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected; no nonlinear, derivative, stochastic-policy or graph-posterior response claim. Temporal ResponseCurve remains single-step; validation cheap/full is not applicable. Same-design and mapped prior transfer may ride via conformance/bayesian/temporal_prior_transfer when every horizon shares the unfolded design; differing I(h) column counts fail closed. Not a matrix axis. The five licensed non-Complete temporal observation pairs instead use estimate.temporal_observed_bayes: Gaussian SEM latent-trajectory Gibbs under declared ignorable joint-trajectory CAR and distinct priors, with pointwise posterior bands and a joint-draw simultaneous credible band. Evidence: crates/antecedent/tests/temporal_observed_bayesian.rs; not Bayesian IPCW/KM/Cox weighting. Repeated-sampling coverage of the InterventionResponse credible bands (400 replicates, n=160, iid and AR(1) rho=0.5 residuals both asserted; measured at nominal 0.95, pointwise and simultaneous: iid 0.948-0.967 (records `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_iid_nominal_95_coverage.set_1_h_1`, `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_iid_nominal_95_coverage.set_1_h_2`, `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.simultaneous_band.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_iid_nominal_95_coverage.simultaneous`), AR(1) 0.944-0.965 (records `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_ar1_nominal_95_coverage.set_1_h_1`, `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_ar1_nominal_95_coverage.set_1_h_2`, `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.simultaneous_band.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_ar1_nominal_95_coverage.simultaneous`) (v19_temporal_response_calibration::bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_{iid,ar1}_nominal_95_coverage); the untempered iid horizon-2 band covered 0.915 (not a registry value) and the AR(1) cell was an unasserted probe): crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_response_calibration.rs. |

Pointwise credible intervals and a simultaneous credible band over the grid (response.simultaneous_band.*; max-studentized deviation of posterior draws; across horizons a product of independent per-horizon posteriors, not a joint horizon posterior) condition on the observed covariate and treatment average. Each horizon's likelihood is tempered by 1/kappa_h (a generalized power posterior; kappa_h is the largest autoregressive-prewhitened (AR(1), plus a BIC-selected AR(q ≤ 4)) Newey-West long-run-variance ratio of that horizon's grid-cell level scores, floored at 1; support diagnostic response.temporal_bayesian.tempering), because lag-aligned h-step residuals are MA(h-1) whenever the outcome or treatment is persistent and serially dependent under autocorrelated innovations; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected; no nonlinear, derivative, stochastic-policy or graph-posterior response claim. Multi-step / joint Sequence overlays use sequential mechanism posteriors (same engine as multi-step Sustained), not bayesian.gcomp. Soft multiplicative and truncated_shift additionally compose across single, joint and multi-step schedules (conformance/response/temporal_soft_mean_mechanisms); truncated_shift defines the population-mean-targeting shift f_policy=f+clip(mu+delta,l,u)-mu, mu=E[f] under preceding interventions, preserving parent effects and innovations; outcomes are not clipped. Nested Sequence remains refused. Validation cheap/full is not applicable. Set / Soft / single-step Sequence may ride prior transfer via conformance/bayesian/temporal_prior_transfer; multi-step Sequence refuses transfer (the catalog filter does not apply to per-mechanism priors). The five licensed non-Complete temporal observation pairs instead use estimate.temporal_observed_bayes: Gaussian SEM latent-trajectory Gibbs under declared ignorable joint-trajectory CAR and distinct priors, with pointwise posterior bands and a joint-draw simultaneous credible band. Evidence: crates/antecedent/tests/temporal_observed_bayesian.rs; not Bayesian IPCW/KM/Cox weighting. Repeated-sampling coverage of the InterventionResponse credible bands (400 replicates, n=160, iid and AR(1) rho=0.5 residuals both asserted; measured at nominal 0.95, pointwise and simultaneous: iid 0.948-0.967 (records `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_iid_nominal_95_coverage.set_1_h_1`, `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_iid_nominal_95_coverage.set_1_h_2`, `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.simultaneous_band.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_iid_nominal_95_coverage.simultaneous`), AR(1) 0.944-0.965 (records `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_ar1_nominal_95_coverage.set_1_h_1`, `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_ar1_nominal_95_coverage.set_1_h_2`, `cov.intervention_response.temporal_dag.bayesian.simultaneous_band.l95.bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_ar1_nominal_95_coverage.simultaneous`) (v19_temporal_response_calibration::bayesian_temporal_dag_intervention_response_{iid,ar1}_nominal_95_coverage); the untempered iid horizon-2 band covered 0.915 (not a registry value) and the AR(1) cell was an unasserted probe): crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_response_calibration.rs. |

| `PointDerivative` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/staged_derivatives`) | none | Complete observations only. Kennedy-DR pseudo-outcome (cross-fitted additive-GAM outcome and homoskedastic Gaussian treatment-density nuisances) smoothed by a Gaussian local quadratic at an explicit caller bandwidth; Silverman's rule is refused. The estimand is the true slope m'(a), not a bandwidth-smoothed surrogate. The point value is the local-quadratic slope. Published intervals are robust bias-corrected (Calonico-Cattaneo-Titiunik, pilot bandwidth = caller bandwidth, numerically the local-cubic slope for order 1 and the local-quartic curvature for order 2, each with its own Eicker-White SE): centered at the bias-corrected slope, not at the local-quadratic point value, with standard_error the bias-corrected SE (runtime diagnostic response.derivative_interval_bias_corrected). The interval treats the cross-fitted pseudo-outcome as data and conditions on the caller bandwidth. Under genuine curvature (m'''(a) != 0) at an MSE-sized bandwidth the pre-1.9 conventional local-quadratic interval covered 0.820 (not a registry value) of nominal 0.90; that interval is replaced, not footnoted. Identity coverage under curvature at nominal 0.90: order 1 0.885 (record `cov.point_derivative.dag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.point_derivative_frequentist_curvature_nominal_90_coverage`) (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_derivative_calibration.rs::point_derivative_frequentist_curvature_nominal_90_coverage); order 2 0.880 (record `cov.point_derivative.dag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.point_derivative_order_2_frequentist_curvature_nominal_90_coverage`) at bandwidth 0.7 (v19_derivative_calibration::point_derivative_order_2_frequentist_curvature_nominal_90_coverage; the previous local-cubic order-2 interval covered 0.695 (not a registry value)). Known truth: linear SCM (tolerance 0.15) and a confounded SCM whose unadjusted Y~A slope is not the structural slope (`conformance/response/staged_derivatives/confounded.json`). No partial-graph, graph-posterior, observation-adjusted, or derivative-native validation license. Local intervals condition on fitted nuisances and bandwidth; Frequentist log-outcome intervals are withheld. |

| `Elasticity` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/staged_derivatives`) | none | Complete observations only. Kennedy-DR pseudo-outcome (cross-fitted additive-GAM outcome and homoskedastic Gaussian treatment-density nuisances) smoothed by a Gaussian local quadratic at an explicit caller bandwidth; Silverman's rule is refused. Known truth: linear SCM (tolerance 0.15); the confounded SCM (`conformance/response/staged_derivatives/confounded.json`) pins only the identity-scale point derivative and ADE. Local intervals condition on fitted nuisances and bandwidth; Frequentist log-outcome intervals are withheld. |

| `AverageDerivative` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/staged_derivatives`) | none | Complete observations only. Observed-law Gaussian-score Riesz ADE with a cross-fitted additive-GAM outcome derivative; custom and uniform weighting are refused. The interval is the influence-function interval and conditions on the fitted nuisances. Coverage on a Gaussian treatment law with a nonlinear outcome: 0.882 (record `cov.average_derivative.dag.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.ade_frequentist_gaussian_treatment_nominal_90_coverage`) at nominal 0.90 (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_derivative_calibration.rs::ade_frequentist_gaussian_treatment_nominal_90_coverage). The representer assumes the treatment is homoskedastic Gaussian given the adjustment set with an additive mean (assumption response.riesz_ade.gaussian_score). That assumption is load-bearing: with a skewed, heteroskedastic treatment law and a nonlinear outcome the measured coverage was 0.180 (not a registry value) (Frequentist) / 0.163 (not a registry value) (Bayesian) at nominal 0.90 (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_derivative_calibration.rs::ade_skewed_heteroskedastic_treatment_probe, recorded, not gated). Known truth: linear additive SCM plus the confounded SCM that separates ADE from the observational slope. No partial-graph, weighted-representer, or derivative-native validation license. |

| `DirectionalDerivative` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/staged_derivatives`) | none | Complete observations only. Additive-GAM plug-in gradients with at most two treatments, a common adjustment set, and a shared complete-case row set; not doubly robust. Treatment smooths are unpenalized cubic regression splines on the nuisance basis (a roughness penalty shrank the published gradient at first order, even for linear components under quantile knots); adjustment smooths keep nuisance_lambda. Differential missingness across outcomes is refused. A directional query is the unnormalized inner product of that gradient with the supplied direction. No Frequentist interval is published. Withheld-interval coordinates are asserted by crates/antecedent/tests/v19_derivative_calibration.rs::frequentist_withheld_derivative_intervals_stay_withheld. Evidence is a correctly specified linear SCM (tolerance 0.15). No partial-graph, graph-posterior, observation-adjusted, or derivative-native validation license. |

| `MediationEffect` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/staged_static_kinds`) | none | Static complete-data DAG. Additive linear parent regressions with independent disturbances and no treatment-mediator interaction; Direct/Mediated aliases denote natural direct/indirect effects in this model. Identification is path-specific natural: a treatment-induced intermediate covariate that is not listed as a mediator and starts both a mediated and an unmediated directed path is a recanting witness, and the query is refused as not identified (as are empty and truncated path sets). Cheap/full run a static mediation-native suite: placebo mediator targets the indirect effect, RCC and subset target the requested contrast. The refuter pin is a deterministic regression check, not an independent statistical oracle. A second confounded SCM (`conformance/estimate/staged_static_kinds/confounded.json`) pins the same structural contrasts against a DGP whose unadjusted parent regressions omit Z. Bayesian inference is licensed in its own cells; no partial-graph or interaction-model license. The Frequentist interval is the pairs-bootstrap SD (se_bootstrap; se_analytic is NaN). Since 1.9 a singular replicate is a counted soft failure (bootstrap_replicates_failed; more than half failed withholds the SE) instead of aborting the run. 400-replicate 90% coverage NDE 0.887 / NIE 0.887 (records `cov.mediation_effect.dag.frequentist.bootstrap_se.l90.mediation_nde_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`, `cov.mediation_effect.dag.frequentist.bootstrap_se.l90.mediation_nie_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) with 200 replicates on a mediator-unconfounded law; with a mediator–outcome confounder in the graph the parent regressions adjust it (probe: 0.905 / 0.917 (not a registry value), bias ≤ 0.006) (coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_calibration.rs::mediation_nde_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage, ::mediation_nie_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage, ::mediation_confounded_mediator_probe, calibration gate). Indirect contrasts report total − direct (the total natural indirect effect) while identification certifies the pure natural indirect effect; they coincide under the no-interaction restriction, recorded as assumption mediation.no_interaction. |
| `MediationEffect` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `cheap` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/staged_static_kinds`) | none | Static complete-data DAG. A second confounded SCM (`conformance/estimate/staged_static_kinds/confounded.json`) pins the same structural contrasts against a DGP whose unadjusted parent regressions omit Z. Bayesian inference is licensed in its own cells; no partial-graph or interaction-model license. The Frequentist interval is the pairs-bootstrap SD (se_bootstrap; se_analytic is NaN). Since 1.9 a singular replicate is a counted soft failure (bootstrap_replicates_failed; more than half failed withholds the SE) instead of aborting the run. 400-replicate 90% coverage NDE 0.887 (record `cov.mediation_effect.dag.frequentist.bootstrap_se.l90.mediation_nde_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) / NIE 0.885 (record `cov.mediation_effect.dag.frequentist.bootstrap_se.l90.mediation_nie_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) with 200 replicates on a mediator-unconfounded law; with a mediator–outcome confounder in the graph the parent regressions adjust it (probe: 0.905 / 0.917 (not a registry value), bias ≤ 0.006) (v19_static_calibration::mediation_*). Indirect contrasts report total − direct (the total natural indirect effect) while identification certifies the pure natural indirect effect; they coincide under the no-interaction restriction, recorded as assumption mediation.no_interaction. |

| `Counterfactual` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/staged_static_kinds`) | none | One-treatment, one-outcome two-world ITE Y(a)-Y(a0) under fitted GCM mechanisms and abducted disturbances, not a factual-versus-counterfactual residual. Control lives on the query (default hard set of treatment to 0). Mechanisms refit on each data click. Sampling uncertainty is unavailable: neither mean_ite nor unit_effects carries an interval (counterfactual.unit_effect_intervals is None; diagnostic gcm.counterfactual.uncertainty_unavailable). Identification is parametric (gcm.parametric). Execution records estimator `gcm.fit`, not linear ATE. A second confounded SCM (`conformance/estimate/staged_static_kinds/confounded.json`) pins mean ITE against a DGP whose unadjusted Y~A association differs. Nested, temporal, graph-posterior, and cheap/full remain refused. Accepted structure shares this estimator and identification; explicit versus accepted execution agrees for the same graph, query, data and seed. Mechanisms are selected per node on validation score from linear-Gaussian, linear_interactions (every cross-parent product) and linear_spline (additive cubic regression spline, three fixed quantile knots per parent with at least five distinct values, plus cross-parent products of every smooth column) for continuous nodes, and from discrete, discrete_interactions and discrete_spline (the same expansions inside the multinomial logit) for categorical nodes; constant is admissible only for a degenerate column. Basis families are scored by deterministic 5-fold (row mod 5) cross-validated error against the incumbent's in-sample error, win only where they predict unseen rows better, and are refused (recorded in failed_families) for a node with fewer than two parents or fewer than 10 rows per expanded column. Selection is invariant to the units of the outcome and of every parent. Parents are standardized inside every basis and parent-conditional categorical fit and back-transformed, so a rescaled parent gives the same mechanism; a categorical fit that still does not converge is refused with reason mechanism_fit_not_converged. The per-unit effects are disclosed as equal by construction (estimate.unit_effects_homogeneous = true, diagnostic gcm.counterfactual.unit_effects_homogeneous, Python homogeneous-mechanism marker) only when every path mechanism is additively separable and no heterogeneity-capable family was successfully fit on those paths; when one was fit and lost, equal effects are an empirical finding and diagnostic gcm.counterfactual.heterogeneity_rejected names the rejected families and scores instead. Diagnostic gcm.counterfactual.support reports the pooled treatment range and, per unit (counterfactual.unit_extrapolative), whether the unit's covariate cell or its abducted additive disturbance lies outside the range observed in the arm it is predicted into; flagged units are still predicted, by the fitted surface's extrapolation. Unit-level ITEs are exact only for invertible additive-noise mechanisms (linear-Gaussian, linear_interactions, linear_spline, hierarchical linear, BVAR, constant, GP predictive mean, dynamic), whose disturbances are recovered by exact inversion (diagnostic gcm.counterfactual reports noise_inference=Invertible). Discrete mechanisms are many-to-one: their disturbance is drawn uniformly within the observed category's CDF bin (noise_inference=PosteriorNoise), and state-space mechanisms draw posterior innovations, so unit effects downstream of those nodes are one sampled counterfactual that varies with the seed, not a point-identified unit ITE. |

Same GCM parametric identification, gcm.fit estimator, and two-world ITE. Unresolved classes, graph-posterior, cheap/full, nested, and temporal remain refused. The per-unit effects are disclosed as equal by construction (estimate.unit_effects_homogeneous = true, diagnostic gcm.counterfactual.unit_effects_homogeneous, Python homogeneous-mechanism marker) only when every path mechanism is additively separable and no heterogeneity-capable family was successfully fit on those paths; when one was fit and lost, equal effects are an empirical finding and diagnostic gcm.counterfactual.heterogeneity_rejected names the rejected families and scores instead.
