| `InterventionResponse` | `CoDetermined` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_cross_check (`crates/antecedent/tests/v15_numeric_pins.rs::codetermined_same_tier_joint_cell_aipw_matches_closure_admg`) | none | Same-tier joint do(T1,T2) on the CoDetermined closure ADMG via Frequentist cell.aipw. Identification is joint ADMG adjustment on the known closure, not a supplied Admg response plug-in. Cheap/full, Bayesian, accepted, and graph-posterior stay n/a. |
| `AverageEffect` | `CoDetermined` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_cross_check (`crates/antecedent/tests/v19_tiered_known_truth.rs::codetermined_average_effect_known_truth`) | none | Binary ATE / ExceedanceGrid on the CoDetermined tier-closure ADMG. Certified Z is the O(p) closure on the background's own closure ADMG, not a supplied Admg search; a caller graph set alongside tiered_background (either order) is CausalError::Conflict, and identify_tiered_on runs the generalized back-door check against a supplied ADMG. Analytic known-truth pin (linear outcome, truth 2) for linear.adjustment.ate and AIPW on fresh and prepared paths; the ExceedanceGrid retarget cross-check stays in v15_numeric_pins.rs. 400-replicate 90% coverage of the AIPW closure interval 0.885 (record `cov.average_effect.co_determined.frequentist.analytic_se.l90.codetermined_aipw_closure_nominal_90_coverage`) (v19_static_envelope_calibration::codetermined_aipw_closure_nominal_90_coverage). Cheap/full, Bayesian, accepted, and graph-posterior stay n/a. |
| `AverageEffect` | `Unknown` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_cross_check (`crates/antecedent/tests/v19_tiered_known_truth.rs::unknown_scenarios_known_truth`) | component oracles: `estimate.linear_regression` (estimator:linear.adjustment.ate) | Two declared orientation scenarios (treatment precedes every same-tier peer: pretreatment set; treatment follows every peer: tier closure), never collapsed. The headline ATE is NaN; scenario_effects / scenario_intervals hold the pair, and the intervals are a 95% simultaneous max-t band from the joint influence-function covariance. Analytic known-truth pin (linear DGP, scenario truths 1 and -1) on fresh and prepared paths; 400-replicate joint coverage of both truths 0.940 (not a registry value) at nominal 95% (v19_static_envelope_calibration::unknown_two_scenario_joint_band_nominal_95_coverage). The scenarios are not exhaustive completions or bounds over the unknown class. Not a Pag ATE. Cheap/full, Bayesian, ConditionalEffect, and Response stay n/a. |
| `PulseEffect` | `TemporalCpdag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/temporal_class_envelope`) | none | Per identified TemporalDag/MAG completion: bayesian_temporal_gcomp. Without ClassPrior the result is an identified set plus atom posteriors, and the scalar set carries identified_set_interval (endpoint quantiles of the per-draw min / max of independently seeded completion posteriors at the Imbens-Manski tail level, so every retained completion's posterior puts at most 1 − Φ(c) of its mass outside each endpoint (not a Frequentist Imbens-Manski interval; flagged truncated when the completion enumeration is capped); calibrated in crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs); enumeration is not mixed. With ClassPrior, aggregate_effect_envelope mixes draws and retains unidentified mass; the blended mean is identified-mass-only under default EnvelopeOptions. A capped completion search cannot confer class-wide identification (full_mass_scope, truncated_atoms) and cannot publish a full-class mixture. Runtime class stays incomplete. cheap/full run envelope refuters/PPC on the mixed contrast. Same-design and mapped prior transfer may ride via conformance/bayesian/temporal_class_prior_transfer; mechanism priors are not a class prior. Pins consume conformance/bayesian/temporal_class_envelope. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. Transfer binds lag-aware coefficient names (coef_<var>@lag<k>): identical-subspace or effect-functional transfer across a different lag, window, or covariate layout, or from an artifact without lag-aware names, fails closed unless an explicit NamedParameters mapping is declared. |

Full prior sensitivity perturbs the prior in force (variance-multiplier grid around a staged or transferred prior, alpha grid for a prior-bank compose, isotropic scale grid only when no prior was supplied); the prior PPC simulates from that prior and the posterior PPC adds a lag-1 residual-autocorrelation (serial) discrepancy. Under full, bootstrap.ci_coverage refits the least-squares contrast on circular blocks of lag-aligned rows as a stand-in for the posterior mean and is not applicable when prior shrinkage separates the two by more than 0.25 posterior SD. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. |

Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. |

Full prior sensitivity perturbs the prior in force (variance-multiplier grid around a staged or transferred prior, alpha grid for a prior-bank compose, isotropic scale grid only when no prior was supplied); the prior PPC simulates from that prior and the posterior PPC adds a lag-1 residual-autocorrelation (serial) discrepancy. Under full, bootstrap.ci_coverage refits the least-squares contrast on circular blocks of lag-aligned rows as a stand-in for the posterior mean and is not applicable when prior shrinkage separates the two by more than 0.25 posterior SD. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. |

Without ClassPrior there is no blended mean; the scalar identified set carries identified_set_interval from the completion posteriors (per-draw min / max of independently seeded completion posteriors at the Imbens-Manski tail level; flagged truncated when the completion enumeration is capped; calibrated in crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs). Pins consume conformance/bayesian/temporal_class_envelope. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory; multi-step windows use each stationary mechanism's contrast gradient), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. |

The cheap suite does not run bootstrap.ci_coverage; under full, multi-step windows check it against the least-squares contrast as a stand-in for the tempered posterior mean (refute.bootstrap.ci_coverage.least_squares_stand_in), and it is NotApplicable under an informative coefficient prior. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. |
| `SustainedEffect` | `TemporalCpdag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `full` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/temporal_class_envelope`) | none | Single-step shares the Bayesian Pulse combiner. Multi-step uses sequential g-comp on directed completions; bidirected MAG completions are unevaluable (mass retained). Without ClassPrior there is no blended mean; the scalar identified set carries identified_set_interval from the completion posteriors (per-draw min / max of independently seeded completion posteriors at the Imbens-Manski tail level; flagged truncated when the completion enumeration is capped; calibrated in crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs). Pins consume conformance/bayesian/temporal_class_envelope. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory; multi-step windows use each stationary mechanism's contrast gradient), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. Full prior sensitivity perturbs the prior in force (variance-multiplier grid around a staged or transferred prior, alpha grid for a prior-bank compose, isotropic scale grid only when no prior was supplied, which is always the case for multi-step windows because they refuse transfer); the prior PPC simulates from that prior and the posterior PPC adds a lag-1 residual-autocorrelation (serial) discrepancy. On multi-step windows bootstrap.ci_coverage resamples circular blocks of lag-aligned rows sized by the Frequentist contrast interval's rule (dependence_block_length over the contrast influence and every mechanism's normal-equation scores) and refits the least-squares contrast as a stand-in for the posterior mean: with each likelihood tempered by 1/kappa under the isotropic prior of scale s, the posterior mean is the ridge fit (X'X + kappa/s^2 I)^-1 X'y, least squares only up to O(kappa/(n s^2)) shrinkage (the check is not applicable when that shrinkage moves the posterior mean more than 0.25 posterior SD from least squares); the refute.bootstrap.ci_coverage.least_squares_stand_in diagnostic discloses this. bootstrap.ci_coverage is NotApplicable under an informative coefficient prior. bootstrap.ci_coverage places the posterior mean inside a least-squares bootstrap interval; it does not test the published credible interval's calibration. |

| `SustainedEffect` | `TemporalPag` | `accepted` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/temporal_class_envelope`) | discovery oracles when the accepted graph is discovered: `discovery.lpcmci`, `pag.discovery.lpcmci` | Single-step shares the Bayesian Pulse combiner. Multi-step uses sequential g-comp on directed completions; bidirected MAG completions are unevaluable (mass retained). Without ClassPrior there is no blended mean; the scalar identified set carries identified_set_interval from the completion posteriors (per-draw min / max of independently seeded completion posteriors at the Imbens-Manski tail level; flagged truncated when the completion enumeration is capped; calibrated in crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_class_calibration.rs). Pins consume conformance/bayesian/temporal_class_envelope. Accepted structure shares the explicit combiner. Credible intervals are a generalized (power) posterior with a serial-dependence correction: each Gaussian likelihood on time-ordered rows is tempered by 1/kappa, kappa = the variance ratio w'Rw/w'w of the effect's combination given the realized design under an AR(q ≤ 4) residual fitted by restricted maximum likelihood (BIC order; partial-autocorrelation parameterization, so the fit is stationary), times the residual-scale factor n/(n − tr(HR)) for the rows the OLS projection removes from the residual sum of squares, times exp(tau²/2) with tau the delta-method spread of log kappa from the REML information; bounded by three times the fixed-b-scaled prewhitened Newey-West ratio of the effect's score (regressor-generated residual structure), floored at 1 and kept at 1 on exact fits (calibrated for short-memory dependence: 400-replicate nominal-90% coverage 0.873–0.907 (not a registry value) for Pulse and Sustained under AR(2)(0.3, 0.5), ARMA(1,1)(0.5, 0.4) and MA(2)(0.6, 0.4) treatment and residual at n = 60–400 (the ARMA(1,1) Pulse n = 160 and AR(2) multi-step Sustained n = 100 cells rechecked at 2000 replicates: 0.888 (record `cov.pulse_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_pulse_arma11_n160_nominal_90_coverage`) and 0.892 (record `cov.sustained_effect.temporal_dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.bayesian_temporal_sustained_multi_ar2_n100_nominal_90_coverage`)), v19_bayesian_temporal::bayesian_temporal_{pulse,sustained_single,sustained_multi}_{ar2,arma11,ma2}_*; not long memory; multi-step windows use each stationary mechanism's contrast gradient), reported by the estimate.bayesian.temporal.dependence_correction diagnostic and a bayes.temporal.long_run_tempering assumption; heteroskedasticity and mean misspecification are not corrected. 400-replicate coverage under iid and AR(1) residuals: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_bayesian_temporal.rs. Every completion or graph atom is fitted under this correction before mixing. |

| `ResponseCurve` | `TemporalCpdag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_cross_check (`crates/antecedent/tests/temporal_class_bayesian_envelope.rs::temporal_class_bayesian_response_is_identified_set`) | none | Per horizon x completion: TemporalResponseEstimator::estimate_bayesian, or sequential mechanism posteriors for Sequence / Soft multiplicative / truncated_shift. Default is an identified set with CompletionEnumeration and estimate.envelope.response_posterior_not_mixed; each atom keeps its own pointwise and simultaneous credible band (conditional on the observed covariate average; estimate_bayesian atoms temper each horizon's likelihood by its long-run-variance ratio, support diagnostic response.temporal_bayesian.tempering), calibrated against that atom's own conditional functional (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_temporal_response_calibration.rs). A ClassPrior publishes conditional_on_identified only when every identified atom is aligned and unidentified mass is zero. Numeric pins: identified-set lower/upper equal the atom posterior means, which match an independent lag-aligned OLS g-computation under each atom's adjustment set (temporal_class_bayesian_response_is_identified_set, ResponseCurve and Set InterventionResponse); this is a cross-check, not a known-truth fixture. cheap/full stay n/a. Same-design and mapped prior transfer may ride via conformance/bayesian/temporal_class_prior_transfer; mechanism priors are not a class prior. |

| `PathSpecificEffect` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/functional_validation`) | none | Functional Bayesian evaluator (one shared Rubin Bayesian-bootstrap Dirichlet(1,...,1)/Exp(1) row law for all identified CPT factors, then the compiled functional). Published distribution atoms are posterior mean probabilities with joint atom draws; a scalar mean posterior is included only when defined. Joint outcomes and full conditional tables retain probability uncertainty without inventing a scalar effect. Identifier stays path_specific.natural or general.id; estimator is functional.effect / functional.distribution, not bayesian.gcomp. Cheap/full reuse the 1.2 path/distribution subset-stability suites. Evidence: conformance/estimate/functional_validation and crates/antecedent/tests/v18_bayesian_remainder.rs. Since 1.9 a directed treatment–outcome path outside the selected set is handled by the edge g-formula (Avin, Shpitser & Pearl 2005; Shpitser 2013): each P(v \| pa(v)) over the outcome's ancestors takes the treatment at the active level on edges that start a selected path and at the control level elsewhere, minus the all-control g-formula, and each Dirichlet draw evaluates that functional on one row law; when every path is selected the effect is the total effect and general ID runs on the surgical graph. A recanting witness (a treatment child that starts both a selected and an unselected directed path, which is exactly the Avin–Shpitser–Pearl criterion; a node shared by paths that leave the treatment through different children is not one) and a truncated path enumeration are refused as not identified. Known truth: conformance/estimate/path_specific_edge_gformula (path effect 0.12, total 0.356; shared-descendant case path effect 0.125, total 0.0625; path_specific_edge_gformula_pins). 400-replicate 90% coverage: chain 0.887 (record `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.frequentist.bootstrap_se.l90.path_specific_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) (Frequentist bootstrap) and 0.895 (record `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.path_specific_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`) (Bayesian); two-path confounded law 0.882 and 0.882 (records `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.path_specific_two_path_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`, `cov.path_specific_effect.dag.frequentist.bootstrap_se.l90.path_specific_two_path_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage`) (coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_calibration.rs::path_specific_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage, ::path_specific_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage, ::path_specific_two_path_frequentist_nominal_90_coverage, ::path_specific_two_path_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage, calibration gate). |

| `MediationEffect` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/staged_static_kinds`) | none | Bayesian linear natural effects: mechanism posterior then natural-effect product. Identifier path_specific.natural, estimator mediation.linear. Pins NDE=1.8 / NIE=4.8 from conformance/estimate/staged_static_kinds. Cheap/full run refute_static_mediation. A shared coefficient prior assigned to both mechanisms is refused. EffectFunctional maps ATE/Δ onto the outcome-mechanism NDE slope only; other mechanisms keep isotropic prior_scale (conformance/bayesian/static_mediation_prior_transfer). Evidence: crates/antecedent/tests/v18_bayesian_remainder.rs. 400-replicate 90% coverage NDE 0.858 (boundary record `cov.mediation_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.mediation_nde_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`) / NIE (product-of-Gaussians posterior) 0.890 (record `cov.mediation_effect.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.mediation_nie_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`) on a mediator-unconfounded law (coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_calibration.rs::mediation_nde_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage, ::mediation_nie_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage, calibration gate). Indirect contrasts report total − direct (the total natural indirect effect) while identification certifies the pure natural indirect effect; they coincide under the no-interaction restriction, recorded as assumption mediation.no_interaction. |

| `Counterfactual` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/staged_static_kinds`) | none | Bayesian bootstrap over fitted GCM mechanisms, then abduction-action-prediction on the original units for every draw. Identifier gcm.parametric, estimator gcm.fit. Pins mean ITE 6.6 from conformance/estimate/staged_static_kinds. Four axes published; unit_effects are the posterior mean of the per-draw unit ITEs. Graph-posterior, cheap/full, nested, and temporal stay refused. Accepted structure shares this estimator; explicit versus accepted execution agrees for the same graph, query, data and seed. Evidence: crates/antecedent/tests/v18_bayesian_remainder.rs. The posterior draws, standard error, and credible interval describe mean_ite, the average contrast over the observed units, and carry mechanism-refit uncertainty only: abducted disturbances are recomputed from the observed rows, so it is not an interval for a population beyond the observed units (diagnostic gcm.counterfactual.bayesian). Family selection is made once on the unweighted data and not revisited per draw. counterfactual.unit_effect_intervals publishes, per observed unit, the equal-tailed 0.95 posterior quantiles of that unit's ITE draws (interval method unit_posterior_quantile): a credible interval for the unit's contrast under the fitted mechanism with mechanism-refit uncertainty only, not a predictive interval for a new unit. Coverage of unit_posterior_quantile and of the mean_ite interval on an interaction DGP (linear_interactions selected) and an exp(z) modifier DGP (linear_spline selected) is measured by crates/antecedent/tests/v110_calibration_counterfactual.rs (calibration gate). 400-replicate 90% coverage 0.873 (boundary record `cov.counterfactual.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.counterfactual_bayesian_mean_ite_boundary_within_band`) on a linear additive SCM (coverage: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_calibration.rs::counterfactual_bayesian_mean_ite_boundary_within_band, calibration gate). Mechanisms are selected per node on validation score from linear-Gaussian, linear_interactions (every cross-parent product) and linear_spline (additive cubic regression spline, three fixed quantile knots per parent with at least five distinct values, plus cross-parent products of every smooth column) for continuous nodes, and from discrete, discrete_interactions and discrete_spline (the same expansions inside the multinomial logit) for categorical nodes; constant is admissible only for a degenerate column. Basis families are scored by deterministic 5-fold (row mod 5) cross-validated error against the incumbent's in-sample error, win only where they predict unseen rows better, and are refused (recorded in failed_families) for a node with fewer than two parents or fewer than 10 rows per expanded column. Selection is invariant to the units of the outcome and of every parent. Parents are standardized inside every basis and parent-conditional categorical fit and back-transformed, so a rescaled parent gives the same mechanism; a categorical fit that still does not converge is refused with reason mechanism_fit_not_converged. The per-unit effects are disclosed as equal by construction (estimate.unit_effects_homogeneous = true, diagnostic gcm.counterfactual.unit_effects_homogeneous, Python homogeneous-mechanism marker) only when every path mechanism is additively separable and no heterogeneity-capable family was successfully fit on those paths; when one was fit and lost, equal effects are an empirical finding and diagnostic gcm.counterfactual.heterogeneity_rejected names the rejected families and scores instead. Diagnostic gcm.counterfactual.support reports the pooled treatment range and, per unit (counterfactual.unit_extrapolative), whether the unit's covariate cell or its abducted additive disturbance lies outside the range observed in the arm it is predicted into; flagged units are still predicted, by the fitted surface's extrapolation. Unit-level ITEs are exact only for invertible additive-noise mechanisms (linear-Gaussian, linear_interactions, linear_spline, hierarchical linear, BVAR, constant, GP predictive mean, dynamic), whose disturbances are recovered by exact inversion (diagnostic gcm.counterfactual reports noise_inference=Invertible). Discrete mechanisms are many-to-one: their disturbance is drawn uniformly within the observed category's CDF bin (noise_inference=PosteriorNoise), and state-space mechanisms draw posterior innovations, so unit effects downstream of those nodes are one sampled counterfactual that varies with the seed, not a point-identified unit ITE. |

| `AverageDerivative` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/staged_derivatives`) | none | Rubin Dirichlet(1,...,1)/Exp(1) row-weight posterior: each draw refits the additive-GAM outcome mu and the Gaussian treatment law alpha on the full weighted sample, then evaluates the weighted Riesz ADE (not a frozen-score reweight); quantile credible interval. Coverage on a Gaussian treatment law with a nonlinear outcome: 0.875 (not a registry value) at nominal 0.90 over 400 replicates and 0.890 (not a registry value) at the 2000-replicate recheck (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_derivative_calibration.rs::ade_bayesian_gaussian_treatment_nominal_90_coverage). The representer assumes the treatment is homoskedastic Gaussian given the adjustment set with an additive mean (assumption response.riesz_ade.gaussian_score). That assumption is load-bearing: with a skewed, heteroskedastic treatment law and a nonlinear outcome the measured coverage was 0.180 (not a registry value) (Frequentist) / 0.163 (not a registry value) (Bayesian) at nominal 0.90 (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_derivative_calibration.rs::ade_skewed_heteroskedastic_treatment_probe, recorded, not gated). Estimator ids stay Riesz/point/GAM; inference is not rewritten to bayesian.gcomp or response.bayesian. Coefficient priors and transfer fail closed (no mapping to the Riesz/local-polynomial/GAM parameterization). Graph-posterior derivative mixtures stay refused. Pins the point value and that the credible interval brackets the truth on conformance/response/staged_derivatives for explicit and accepted structure (crates/antecedent/tests/v18_bayesian_remainder.rs::derivative_bayesian_pins). |

| `DirectionalDerivative` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/staged_derivatives`) | none | Rubin Dirichlet(1,...,1)/Exp(1) row-weight posterior: each draw refits the additive regression spline under the draw's weights with fixed knots and penalty; pointwise quantile bands. Additive-GAM plug-in gradients with at most two treatments, a common adjustment set, and a shared complete-case row set; not doubly robust. Treatment smooths are unpenalized cubic regression splines on the nuisance basis (a roughness penalty shrank the published gradient at first order, even for linear components under quantile knots); adjustment smooths keep nuisance_lambda. Differential missingness across outcomes is refused. A directional query is the unnormalized inner product of that gradient with the supplied direction. Per-coordinate coverage on an additive SCM with a quadratic component: 0.880, 0.878 (not a registry value) at nominal 0.90 over 2000 replicates (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_derivative_calibration.rs::directional_derivative_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage, a named boundary cell). The band covers the derivative when the additive components lie in, or are well approximated by, the cubic-spline space; sieve approximation bias is not inside it. Estimator ids stay Riesz/point/GAM; inference is not rewritten to bayesian.gcomp or response.bayesian. Coefficient priors and transfer fail closed (no mapping to the Riesz/local-polynomial/GAM parameterization). Graph-posterior derivative mixtures stay refused. Pins the point value and that the credible interval brackets the truth on conformance/response/staged_derivatives for explicit and accepted structure (crates/antecedent/tests/v18_bayesian_remainder.rs::derivative_bayesian_pins). |

| `Elasticity` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/staged_derivatives`) | none | Rubin Dirichlet(1,...,1)/Exp(1) row-weight posterior of the Kennedy-DR point derivative: every draw refits the cross-fitted additive-GAM outcome and Gaussian treatment nuisances on its weighted training folds and rebuilds the pseudo-outcome (not a frozen reweight); held fixed across draws are only the caller bandwidth, fold assignment, spline knots, and penalty (assumption bayesian.derivative.estimator_bootstrap and diagnostic response.derivative_interval_bias_corrected say so). The value is the posterior mean of the weighted local-quadratic coordinate; the credible interval and standard_error come from the per-draw robust bias-corrected coordinate (local-cubic slope; local-quartic level and curvature) at the same bandwidth, and scale transforms are applied per draw. Unlike the Frequentist cell, elasticity publishes this draw-based interval because the per-draw transform carries the joint level/slope uncertainty. Coverage under curvature: 0.899 (record `cov.elasticity.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.elasticity_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`) at nominal 0.90 (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_derivative_calibration.rs::elasticity_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage). Estimator ids stay Riesz/point/GAM; inference is not rewritten to bayesian.gcomp or response.bayesian. Coefficient priors and transfer fail closed (no mapping to the Riesz/local-polynomial/GAM parameterization). Graph-posterior derivative mixtures stay refused. Pins the point value and that the credible interval brackets the truth on conformance/response/staged_derivatives for explicit and accepted structure (crates/antecedent/tests/v18_bayesian_remainder.rs::derivative_bayesian_pins). Log-treatment scales require a positive evaluation point. |

Additive-GAM plug-in gradients with at most two treatments, a common adjustment set, and a shared complete-case row set; not doubly robust. Treatment smooths are unpenalized cubic regression splines on the nuisance basis (a roughness penalty shrank the published gradient at first order, even for linear components under quantile knots); adjustment smooths keep nuisance_lambda. Differential missingness across outcomes is refused. The published value is the row-major Jacobian of outcomes with respect to treatments. Per-coordinate coverage on an additive SCM with a quadratic component: 0.871, 0.883, 0.894, 0.878 (not a registry value) at nominal 0.90 over 2000 replicates, below the gate's precision floor: the band is centred but 4–5% narrow (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_derivative_calibration.rs::response_jacobian_bayesian_boundary_within_band, a named boundary cell). The band covers the derivative when the additive components lie in, or are well approximated by, the cubic-spline space; sieve approximation bias is not inside it. Estimator ids stay Riesz/point/GAM; inference is not rewritten to bayesian.gcomp or response.bayesian. Coefficient priors and transfer fail closed (no mapping to the Riesz/local-polynomial/GAM parameterization). Graph-posterior derivative mixtures stay refused. Pins the point value and that the credible interval brackets the truth on conformance/response/staged_derivatives for explicit and accepted structure (crates/antecedent/tests/v18_bayesian_remainder.rs::derivative_bayesian_pins). |

| `SemiElasticity` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Bayesian` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/staged_derivatives`) | none | Rubin Dirichlet(1,...,1)/Exp(1) row-weight posterior of the Kennedy-DR point derivative: every draw refits the cross-fitted additive-GAM outcome and Gaussian treatment nuisances on its weighted training folds and rebuilds the pseudo-outcome (not a frozen reweight); held fixed across draws are only the caller bandwidth, fold assignment, spline knots, and penalty (assumption bayesian.derivative.estimator_bootstrap and diagnostic response.derivative_interval_bias_corrected say so). The value is the posterior mean of the weighted local-quadratic coordinate; the credible interval and standard_error come from the per-draw robust bias-corrected coordinate (local-cubic slope; local-quartic level and curvature) at the same bandwidth, and scale transforms are applied per draw. Coverage under curvature: log_scale=treatment 0.899, log_scale=outcome 0.899 (records `cov.semi_elasticity.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.semi_elasticity_log_outcome_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`, `cov.semi_elasticity.dag.bayesian.posterior_quantile.l90.semi_elasticity_log_treatment_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage`) at nominal 0.90 (crates/antecedent/tests/v19_derivative_calibration.rs::semi_elasticity_log_treatment_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage, semi_elasticity_log_outcome_bayesian_nominal_90_coverage); the log-outcome interval is published here although the Frequentist cell withholds it. Estimator ids stay Riesz/point/GAM; inference is not rewritten to bayesian.gcomp or response.bayesian. Coefficient priors and transfer fail closed (no mapping to the Riesz/local-polynomial/GAM parameterization). Graph-posterior derivative mixtures stay refused. Pins the point value and that the credible interval brackets the truth on conformance/response/staged_derivatives for explicit and accepted structure (crates/antecedent/tests/v18_bayesian_remainder.rs::derivative_bayesian_pins). Log-treatment scales require a positive evaluation point. |

| `ConditionalEffect` | `Dag` | `graph_posterior` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | component oracles: `estimate.identify.backdoor` (identifier:backdoor.adjustment), `estimate.conditional` (estimator:conditional.linear.adjustment) | Per-atom conditional identification on DAG graph-posterior atoms. Published moments are E[τ \| identified] (identified-atom BMA); unidentified mass is a separate non-renormalized axis (assumption envelope.conditional_on_identification). Frequentist uses conditional.linear.adjustment; Bayesian uses conditional.bayesian and is not a silent Frequentist reuse. Cheap/full run the refuters on each contributing atom against that atom's own CATE and mix the reports by graph mass; the mixed check passes only if every atom passes. Evidence: crates/antecedent/tests/v18_bayesian_remainder.rs and conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures. With more than one contributing atom the Frequentist SE is the joint influence-function covariance of the atoms' CATE IFs on shared rows with frozen graph weights (estimate.graph_posterior.joint_if_se); each atom's CATE influence function includes the sampling variance of the modifier mean. A single contributing atom keeps its own SE; unalignable atoms give NaN plus estimate.envelope.se_omits_between_atom_variance. 400-replicate coverage with atoms whose CATEs differ: crates/antecedent/tests/v19_static_mixture_calibration.rs. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |

| `ConditionalEffect` | `Dag` | `graph_posterior` | `Bayesian` | `full` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | component oracles: `estimate.identify.backdoor` (identifier:backdoor.adjustment) | Per-atom conditional identification on DAG graph-posterior atoms. Prior sensitivity perturbs the prior in force (variance-multiplier grid around a staged or transferred prior, alpha grid for a prior-bank compose, isotropic scale grid only when no prior was supplied; the summary records the family) and the prior PPC simulates from that prior. Under the interactive latency tier at most 16 identified atoms are fitted (a stratified subsample, estimate.envelope.interactive_subsample); the mass of the atoms not fitted is reported separately as subsampled-out mass (in the envelope diagnostic, and as subsampled_out_mass on the structured response or posterior where the cell publishes one), not as unidentified or unevaluable mass, and the result is GraphDependent. |
| `InterventionalDistribution` | `Admg` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/admg_frontdoor_distribution`) | component oracles: `estimate.identify.backdoor` (identifier:general.id), `estimate.identify.general_id` (identifier:general.id) | Unconditional finite-discrete tables only. General ID on the supplied ADMG; bidirected edges are not dropped to coerce a Dag. Validation none only; cheap/full and IDC conditionals are refused. A nonidentified ADMG refuses rather than publishing a table. Prepared clicks reuse the cached functional. No continuous density claim. Known truth: binary front-door SCM with latent U (conformance/estimate/admg_frontdoor_distribution), P(Y=1\|do(T=1)) = 0.625 and P(Y=1\|do(T=0)) = 0.375 on the exact 800-row law; the plug-in matches to 1e-12 on fresh and prepared paths, and with 100 bootstrap replicates the logit-bounded probability interval is finite and brackets it. The interval is not coverage-calibrated. Python `analyze`/`prepare` reach this cell. |

Frozen posterior weights via StructuralAggregationPolicy (estimate.graph_posterior.structural_aggregation). Each identified atom uses its own S(h) (its I(h) plus mediator-outcome confounders); sets are not unioned. Total, Direct and Mediated are fixed-weight means over evaluable atoms. When replicates are requested, one shared circular-block replicate over the lag-aligned series times every atom can evaluate refits every atom's three mechanism regressions and mixes them with the frozen weights inside the replicate, so the three SEs are for the reported aggregate and include between-atom covariance; the block length is dependence-aware (at least max(span, ⌈m^{1/3}⌉), lengthened on persistent contrast or normal-equation scores) and the replicate SD carries the circular-Bartlett fixed-b factor; estimate.temporal.circular_block_se.short_series warns below the temporal mediation threshold (one atom) or the mixture threshold. With one identified atom this is that atom's own block bootstrap. Without usable replicates the aggregate SE is withheld (estimate.dbn_posterior.mediation.uncertainty_withheld); no iid SE is substituted. Identified/unidentified/unevaluable mass, atom values and the atom range are in structural_response (posterior_probability weights); unidentified or unevaluable mass makes the status GraphDependent. Failed estimation is unevaluable, not unidentified. The SE is checked as finite and positive, not calibrated for coverage. Python: analyze() and PreparedAnalysis.prepare(discovery=DbnPosterior(...) or GraphPosterior(...)) with Frequentist() reach this cell; without an explicit bootstrap the replicate count follows the latency tier (interactive 0, standard 199, report 200; 199 without a tier). cheap/full are licensed separately: mediation refuters run per contributing atom against that atom's own contrast and mix by fixed graph weight. Class-valued posterior atoms remain refused. |
| `TemporalMediationEffect` | `TemporalDag` | `graph_posterior` | `Frequentist` | `cheap` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/bayesian/known_truth_mixtures`) | component oracles: `estimate.temporal_mediation.external_parity` (estimator:temporal.mediation) | Same frozen mediation mixture as none: mediated E[tau \| identified]=0.44 with 0.3 unidentified mass retained and per-atom S(h). Cheap runs the mediation suite (placebo-mediator, RCC, empirical binary mediator-range overlap when applicable) on every contributing atom against that atom's own contrast using that atom's S(h), not the pooled mean, and mixes reports by fixed graph weight; the mixed check passes only if every atom passes. They do not rediscover graphs. Interval calibration is unchanged from the none cell. |

Full adds contiguous-window stability to the cheap per-atom mediation refuters, mixed by fixed graph weight; the mixed check passes only if every atom passes. They do not rediscover graphs. Interval calibration is unchanged from the none cell. |

| `AnomalyAttribution` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/staged_attribution`) | none | GCM anomaly scores on a supplied explicit Dag. Identification is parametric (gcm.parametric); estimator gcm.fit. Not an average treatment effect. Sampling uncertainty is unavailable. cheap/full, Bayesian, accepted, graph-posterior, and non-Dag graphs remain refused. Python analyze(data, graph=Dag, query=AnomalyAttribution(...)) and the Rust Study API run this cell and retain a study; a second-click refuter suite refuses with option_not_applicable. |
| `ChangeAttribution` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/staged_attribution`) | none | GCM distribution-change Shapley on a supplied explicit Dag, two time-range populations. Identification is parametric (gcm.parametric); estimator gcm.fit. Pins total_change from conformance/estimate/staged_attribution (same two-period intercept-shift chain as conformance/attribution/distribution_change_y_shift). cheap/full, Bayesian, accepted, graph-posterior, and non-Dag graphs remain refused. Python analyze(data, graph=Dag, query=ChangeAttribution(...)) and the Rust Study API run this cell and retain a study; a second-click refuter suite refuses with option_not_applicable. |
| `TransportQuery` | `Admg` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/estimate/staged_transport`) | component oracles: `identify.transport_sid` (identifier:transport.sid) | Single-source selection diagram on an explicit Admg. Identification is the implemented sID subset (Direct or S-admissible standardize). This cell estimates the binary trial-to-target ATE via Dahabreh IPW from caller-supplied trial membership and known selection/treatment probabilities, with the delta-method SE of that ratio-of-means over iid rows; the inner ResponseCurve names treatment/outcome only and is not a dose-response estimate. RecursiveFactorization and NotCertified refuse. A derivative or non-mean inner response, an embedded population, observation mechanism or temporal attachment, and RecursiveFactorization refuse with construction_not_licensed; NotCertified refuses at identification. cheap/full, Bayesian, accepted, graph-posterior, and non-Admg graphs remain refused. Selection diagram and trial column bindings freeze at prepare; each execution reads the trial columns from its own data. Python analyze(data, graph=Admg, query=TransportQuery(..., trial=, selection_probability=, treatment_probability=)) and the Rust Study API run this cell and retain a study; retarget refuses with population_not_estimable and a second-click refuter suite with option_not_applicable. The calibration slot is keyed TransportQuery/Admg/fixed/tabular/Frequentist/transport.trial_ipw/analytic_se/iid at the reported 0.95 level. |
| `InterferenceQuery` | `Dag` | `explicit` | `Frequentist` | `none` | internal_known_truth (`conformance/response/randomized_interference`) | none | Design-based Horvitz–Thompson / Hájek exposure contrast. The Dag binds schema/outcome; it does not identify the contrast. Licensed mapping is NeighborCount under Bernoulli assignment on a caller-supplied fixed network. Variance is the conservative Young bound, not Aronow–Samii. Complete or cluster randomization and every other exposure mapping refuse with construction_not_licensed. cheap/full, Bayesian, accepted, graph-posterior, and non-Dag graphs remain refused. The network and realized assignment freeze at prepare; the unit table is the data, so a refresh executes on new outcomes under the frozen design. Python analyze(data, graph=Dag or edges, query=InterferenceQuery(..., network=, realized_assignment=)) and the Rust Study API run this cell and retain a study; retarget refuses with population_not_estimable and a second-click refuter suite with option_not_applicable. The calibration slot is keyed InterferenceQuery/Dag/fixed/tabular/Frequentist/interference.ht_hajek/analytic_se/iid at the reported 0.95 level. |
